**Cross-references from a tab to parent state/methods** go through `this.app.X`:
- `this.app._saveScroll()` — preserves admin-content scroll
- `this.app._activeTab` — currently active tab key
- `this.app._animationActive` — suppress debounced renders during multi-step writes
- `this.app._relativeTime(iso)`, `this.app._getRelativeTime(ts)` — formatters
- `this.app.constructor._findItem(id)` — static item lookup (used by ShardsTab, NetworksTab)

## Architecture: GM Admin Panel theme (separate from player theme)

The Admin Panel has its **own** theme, isolated from the player theme. The player `ThemeService` writes `--ncm-*` to `:root`; `styles/themes/admin-theme.css` re-defines the same `--ncm-*` variables **on any element carrying `data-ncm-admin-theme`** (`:is(.ncm-app, .ncm-admin-dialog)[data-ncm-admin-theme]`; the bare `.ncm-app.ncm-admin-panel` is kept as a robust default), which wins for every descendant (custom properties resolve from the nearest ancestor) — so each tagged window is themed in isolation and `:root` is untouched. **Don't move these overrides to `:root`** (that's the opposite of the player-theme rule) and **don't scope them tighter than the window root** or descendants won't inherit them.

**Carry-over to every GM tool (2026-09-10, tester: "Light theme, open Full Editor → still dark").** `scripts/utils/adminTheme.js` is the ONE stamping path. Rule agreed with the dev: **GM tools follow the admin theme + text size; player-facing immersive windows (message viewer, composer, shard viewer, terminal, Contact Manager, Theme Customizer, Program Deck) stay on the player theme even on a GM client.**
- **AppV2 windows opt in with `static ADMIN_THEMED = true`** (BaseApplication default false). `BaseApplication._onRender` calls `_applyAdminTheme()` right after `_applyAtmosphere` (stamps the attribute, forces scanlines/neon off, applies the text-size zoom + one-time frame resize via `_applyAdminTextScale`). Flagged: AdminPanelApp, GMContactManagerApp, ActorImportPickerApp, ContactShareDialog, NetworkManagementApp, CreateNetworkDialog, TerminalConfigApp, ItemInboxConfig, DataShardComposer (its `_onRender` skips super, so it calls `this._applyAdminTheme()` explicitly — keep that), MalwareWorkshopApp. A new GM window = add the flag; a subclass that overrides `_onRender` without super must call it by hand.
- **Foundry v1 `Dialog`s can't inherit a window's vars (body-mounted), so GM code opens them through `adminDialog` / `adminConfirm` / `adminPrompt` / `adminWait`** (same signatures as `new Dialog` / `Dialog.confirm` / `Dialog.prompt` / `Dialog.wait`), which add the `ncm-admin-dialog` class; the `renderDialog` hook (`registerAdminDialogHook`, registerGMTools ready/111) stamps the attribute + zoom and grows the width once. admin-theme.css carries explicit chrome rules for `.ncm-admin-dialog[data-ncm-admin-theme]` (frame/header/content/inputs/buttons, `!important` against core + CPR). All ~80 GM-tool call sites were swept (AdminPanel tabs, GM Contact Manager, Role Manager, Terminal/Shard config, entry composer, Network Manager, Workshop, ShardSheetOverride, DateRangePicker; `showWorldItemPicker` passes `admin:true` while the player inventory picker stays plain). **A `new Dialog(` in GM-tool code is a bug** — grep for it. **Player-facing windows have their own wrappers (2026-10-21, tester: "pop-up windows still not resizing for text")** — `playerDialog` / `playerConfirm` / `playerPrompt` / `playerWait` (same file) tag `ncm-player-dialog`; the same `renderDialog` hook zooms those by the PLAYER's Theme Customizer Text Size (read live from `:root`'s `--ncm-text-scale-base`, saved `playerTheme.textScale` as fallback, clamped to the slider's 80–150) and grows the width once, with **no palette change** (a body-mounted Dialog keeps Foundry's look by design). **A dialog follows its OPENER**: when `document.activeElement` sits inside a `[data-ncm-admin-theme]` window (GM-view viewer/composer/contacts, a config sheet) the player wrapper tags it admin instead — the CyberTimePicker rule, now generic (the picker's own `activeElement` check is gone). The 30 raw call sites in the viewer / composer / Contact Manager / shard viewer / terminal / Theme Customizer / CyberTimePicker / `showNamePicker({admin:false})` were swept; **a raw `new Dialog(` / `Dialog.confirm(` anywhere in `scripts/` is now a dialog that scales with nothing** — `tools/sims/test-admin-theme.mjs` (61 checks) walks the tree and fails on one (exempt: the wrappers and the namespace-migration prompt, which runs before the hook exists). Not live-tested.
- **Brand hex → `--ncm-*` sweep**: `node tools/sweep-brand-hex.mjs [--check] [--only <selector regex>] <css…>` rewrites the literal brand palette (variables.css defaults: `#19f3f7`→secondary, `#2a2a45`→border, `#1a1a2e`→bg-surface, `#555570`→text-muted, brand `rgba()`→`color-mix`) to variables; it skips `var(--x, #hex)` fallbacks and non-brand colours. Swept: gm-contact-manager, network-management, shard-config, create-network-dialog, role-manager, activity-log, actor-import-picker, cyber-time-picker, tab-tools/tab-security dialogs, and item-inbox.css **composer blocks only** (`--only`, so the player shard viewer + `.shard-preset-*` tokens are untouched). `--nm-net-*` network-TYPE colours were restored by hand — they're identity, not theme (standing rule). `--check` exits 1 if a swept file regains a brand literal. Malware Workshop needed nothing (its hexes are all `var(…, #fallback)`).
- **The sweep table has THREE tiers, and the first pass alone left "the configs looking rough" (dev, same day).** Tier 1 = the variables.css brand palette. Tier 2 = the near-brand neutrals the config sheets typed by hand (`#0e0e16`/`#0a0a12` section backgrounds, `#5a5a7a`…`#d0d0d8` greys) — left alone they stay near-black on the light preset while the text flips dark. Tier 3 = the terminal config's account editor shades in terminal.css (`#0b0b14`, `#22223a`, `#dde`, `#eef`…). Border declarations map neutrals to `--ncm-border-active` (`BORDER_OVERRIDES`). The tool also rewrites hardcoded `'Rajdhani'`/`'Share Tech Mono'`/`'Orbitron'` stacks to the `--ncm-font-*` slots. **Deliberately NOT mapped:** the boot/login/screensaver PREVIEW stages (`#050508`, `#07070c`…) — they mimic a terminal screen and must stay dark under any preset; status colours (trust-segment orange/green, locked purple, ICE red); `--*-net-*` declarations (`KEEP_DECL`). Guard placeholders are `@@G0@@`-style — the first version used ` 0 ` and would have swallowed `flex: 1 1 auto` (audited: no file was hit).
- Probe: `docs/mockups/admin-theme-carryover-preview.html?t=clean-light|clean-dark|high-contrast&zoom=130` renders a tagged AppV2 window + a tagged Dialog + an UNTAGGED control dialog against the real cascade and dumps computed vars into `<title>`/`#report`. **`docs/mockups/config-theme-probe.html?tpl=shard|terminal&t=…&tab=accounts`** renders the REAL `item-inbox-config.hbs` / `terminal-config.hbs` (partials auto-registered by their Foundry path names) through Handlebars with a permissive Proxy context (every branch shows, values print their path — a palette check, not a data check; the Proxy must use an object target + own-property descriptors + `toHTML`→undefined or Handlebars reads every value as empty) and reports low-contrast text nodes in `<title>`. Ignore hits whose background prints as `color(srgb … / 0.0x)` — translucent tints, not surfaces. Sim: `tools/sims/test-admin-theme.mjs` (45 checks: wrappers, stamp/zoom, hook, flag inventory, immersive windows unflagged, CSS scope).
- Text size = CSS `zoom` on `.window-content` + frame growth (GM stylesheets are ~fixed-px with no `--ncm-text-scale`); `adminTheme.textScale` 100–150 via the Tools-tab chips.
- **GM view toggle (2026-09-10, dev: "toggle GM view / player view in the inboxes… the composer too, for readability").** Immersive windows are NOT `ADMIN_THEMED`, but `MessageViewerApp`, `MessageComposerApp` and `ContactManagerApp` carry **`static GM_VIEW_TOGGLE = true`**: a GM gets a header ⋮-menu entry ("Switch to GM view / player view") that flips the **client** setting `gmViewAdminTheme` (default **on**) and re-renders every open toggle window; on = admin preset + text size stamped on that window (`applyGmView`), off = the player theme (what the players see). Never affects players. Plumbing lives in BaseApplication (`_getHeaderControls` + `_onClickAction` + the `_onRender` branch) — **the composer does NOT extend BaseApplication**, so it mirrors those three hooks by hand; keep them in step. A subclass that overrides `_onClickAction` must call super or the toggle dies.
- **Third round (same day, GM-view live test) — four more, all now standing rules:** (1) **`_getHeaderControls` must return a COPY** — `super` hands back the shared `options.window.controls` array, and pushing into it appended one more "Switch to…" entry per render (the doubled menu item). (2) **AppV2 builds the frame ONCE, so a header-menu label never re-renders** — the toggle is therefore the injected **`.ncm-gmview-btn` header button** beside the close control (lit in the admin accent while GM view is on), patched by `syncGmViewControls` (adminTheme.js) on every render/toggle. **There is deliberately NO ⋮-menu entry** (dev: the eye button does the job; the menu duplicated it). JS-built dialogs with inline styles must use vars too — `DateRangePicker` (Tools → date range) was swept like MessagesTab's View Inbox. **Viewer pane stacking:** both panes are `isolation: isolate` stacking contexts, so the Dates/Sort/Filters dropdowns (z-index 30 inside the LIST pane) slid under the later-in-DOM DETAIL pane; message-viewer.css orders them (`list-panel z-index:2`, `detail-panel z-index:1`). (3) **The viewer/contacts/composer sheets carried a fourth palette** — Tailwind-ish `#00d4e6`/`#ef4444`/`#a78bfa`/`#34d399`/`#fbbf24` + neutral rgba triplets (`224,224,232`…) + `color:#fff` for the subject/header — tier 4 in the sweep tool (incl. a `WHITE_TEXT` rule: `color:#fff` → `--ncm-text-primary`, `color:` only). **NEAR-white was the hole in that rule** (2026-09-21): it matched pure white only, so `#f0f0f5` on `.ncm-viewer__subject-text` and `#f0f0f8` on the composer's bold text survived — and a near-white that never flips is exactly as unreadable on `clean-light` as a pure one. The message SUBJECT measured **1.05:1** against the light background (the dev: "white on white") and now reads 14.94:1. `WHITE_TEXT` now takes any `color:` literal whose channels are all ≥ 0xE0, and **`--check` reports them** — it used to scan only the brand HEX/RGB maps, so even a pure `color:#fff` could be reintroduced silently. Probe: **`docs/mockups/gmview-contrast-probe.html?t=clean-light|clean-dark|high-contrast|player`** renders the real markup against the real cascade and puts the worst text/background contrast ratio in `<title>` (it walks UP for the first opaque background — judging text against the translucent tint it sits on reports a false pass).. Players see no change (the vars resolve to the same brand colours under the player theme); under GM view the accents follow the preset. Swept: message-viewer / message-detail / message-viewer-hud / viewer-network-states / network-selector / contact-manager / message-composer / composer-atmosphere / theme-customizer / tab-messages / contact-verification / cyber-time-picker / **shard-sheet-badge** (it owns the `.ncm-pick-dialog` chrome that the email-domain and other pick dialogs wear — a class-collision source, all literal brand). (4) **Body-mounted popups inherit nothing** — `CyberTimePicker` tags its Dialog `ncm-admin-dialog` when `document.activeElement` sits inside a `[data-ncm-admin-theme]` window (a GM-view composer, a config sheet), so the picker matches its opener. **Theme Customizer** carries `GM_VIEW_TOGGLE` too; its `_applyLive` must skip `[data-ncm-admin-theme]` windows (it re-stamped scanlines onto the Admin Panel) and `_scopePreviewToPlayerTheme` re-applies the working prefs element-scoped onto `.ncm-tc__preview`/`.ncm-tc__fprev` so the preview shows the PLAYER palette while the chrome is admin. **Shard and terminal viewers deliberately have NO toggle** (dev: "I don't think the toggle needs to be on the shards"). Probe: `config-theme-probe.html?tpl=message-viewer/message-viewer.hbs&cls=ncm-message-viewer-window&f=isDeadZone,isEncrypted,…` — `?f=` forces named `{{#if}}` keys false so overlay states don't bury the list.
- **Second "still looks off" round (same day) — three token/scope bugs the sweep can't see:** (1) the Malware Workshop's rails/headers used **`--ncm-bg-mid`, which was never defined anywhere**, so 15 rules fell to their dark-slate fallback under every preset — now in variables.css (`#161622`) and all three admin presets. Run the undefined-var check (node one-liner in the session: every `var(--ncm-…)` name vs variables.css + admin-theme.css) when a themed window "ignores" the preset. (2) The **`--sp-*` shard tokens are defined on the shard viewer root only**; the entry composer and the workshop reuse `.ncm-shard-btn`/`.ncm-cl-*`/`.ncm-config-*` and got the dark fallbacks — item-inbox.css now mirrors the token block onto `.ncm-app.ncm-data-shard-composer, .ncm-app.ncm-malware-workshop`. (3) **JS-built dialogs with INLINE `style="color:#e0e0e8"`** (Messages → View Inbox) ignore every stylesheet — converted to `var(--ncm-*)`; grep `style="[^"]*#[0-9a-f]{6}` in `scripts/ui/AdminPanel` before adding one. Also: **scanline films drawn by `::after` regardless of the atmosphere attribute** (`.ncm-netmgr::after`, `.ncm-create-network-dialog::after`) are gated on `[data-ncm-scanlines="false"]` in admin-theme.css ("get rid of scanlines in subnet control"). **Heat rows name their object via `SecurityTab._heatObjectName`** (world item → actor-owned item "(owner)" → network → uuid → "(deleted object · id…)") — it used to print a raw id for anything but a world item. **Probe for JS-built windows:** `docs/mockups/workshop-theme-probe.html?t=…&lib=1` stubs enough Foundry to import the REAL `MalwareWorkshopApp`, construct it, and `_mount()` into the real cascade (the hbs is a 3-line shell, so the template probe shows nothing).

- **Presets are pure CSS** keyed by `data-ncm-admin-theme="clean-dark|clean-light|high-contrast"` (no per-variable JS injection). `clean-dark` is the base rule (applies with no attr too, so it's the robust default); light/high-contrast are attribute overrides declaring only their palette diffs (glow/font overrides fall through from the base rule). Goal: clarity/accessibility/cleanliness — a calm slate dashboard, one restrained accent, **no neon/scanlines**, legible system UI fonts (mono kept for numbers).
- `AdminPanelApp._applyAdminTheme()` (called in `_onRender` **after** `super._onRender` so it beats `_applyAtmosphere`) sets the `data-ncm-admin-theme` attr from the `adminTheme` setting **and** force-sets `data-ncm-scanlines/neon="false"` regardless of player prefs.
- **Settings:** client-scoped `adminTheme` (`{ preset }`, `DEFAULTS.ADMIN_THEME`), with `SettingsManager.getAdminTheme/setAdminTheme` — fully independent of `playerTheme`.
- **Picker:** Tools tab "Admin Appearance" (`setAdminTheme` action → `ToolsTab.onSetAdminTheme` saves + re-renders; `ToolsTab.prepareContext` provides the preset list + active state).
- **Text size (2026-09-10, accessibility):** `adminTheme.textScale` (100/115/130/150, `ToolsTab.ADMIN_TEXT_SCALES`, chips under the preset picker → `setAdminTextScale`). The panel's CSS is ~500 fixed-px font sizes with no `--ncm-text-scale` plumbing, so `AdminPanelApp._applyAdminTextScale` sets a CSS **`zoom`** on `.window-content` and grows the window frame by the same ratio (tracked in `_appliedTextScale`) so the 820px layout keeps its proportions. Player-facing windows scale through the Theme Customizer's Text Size slider instead (range raised 120 → **150**).
- **Window-chrome controls follow the admin theme too.** Foundry colors the `.window-header .header-control` **icons** (`<i>`, close/etc.) near-white by default, and `window-chrome.css`'s `.header-control { color }` rule doesn't reach the inner `<i>`. That's invisible on the dark presets but **white-on-white on `clean-light`**. `admin-theme.css` forces both the button and its `> i` to `--ncm-text-secondary` (hover `--ncm-text-primary`; close-hover `--ncm-danger`), **scoped to `.ncm-app.ncm-admin-panel`** so the player theme's chrome is untouched.

**The admin panel has ONE visual vocabulary — copy it, don't invent one.** Both new tabs (Security, Terminals) shipped feeling foreign and the dev flagged it as "the fonts look off… doesn't match the other tabs". It measured out as two objective inversions of the shipped convention, so check yours before adding a tab:
- **Fonts: display-dominant chrome, mono for VALUES ONLY.** `grep -o "var(--ncm-font-[a-z]*" styles/components/admin-panel/tab-X.css | sort | uniq -c` — the shipped tabs run display:mono ≈ **59:5** (contacts), 31:3 (shards), 49:21 (messages). The new tabs were **inverted** (security 4:19, terminals 2:14) — mono on buttons/labels/badges/titles, which reads as a different app. This is just the standing font rule (Rajdhani chrome, Share Tech Mono for *technical data values*) applied: a person's name = display, a machine/file/ID/timestamp/roll/count = mono.
- **Radius is 2px. Everywhere.** Shipped tabs: 28×2px (contacts), 23×2px (shards), 31×2px (messages), 32×2px (networks). The new tabs used 5–9px + a 20px pill — rounded reads as a foreign UI next to sharp neighbours. Badges are **1px**.
- **Copy these three, they are the reference implementations:** button = `.ncm-spam-send-btn` (display 11/700, uppercase, `letter-spacing:.04em`, radius 2px, `appearance:none`, `min-height:0`); badge/chip = `.ncm-ct-badge` (display 9/700 uppercase .04em, radius 1px); **checkbox = `.ncm-ct-row__check`** (outlined box, radius 2px, checked = faint `secondary` fill + a **cyan ✓**). Note the module-global `.ncm-app input[type=checkbox]` (base/interactions.css) paints a *solid cyan box with a dark border-tick* — right for the player UI, foreign in the panel; override it per-surface. **`--checkbox-size` does NOT work for this** — Foundry core declares that var ON the input, so a value inherited from a wrapping label loses; size the box directly and neutralise BOTH the module's border-tick geometry and core's layered FA-glyph `content:"\f14a\f14a"`.
- Section titles: display 11–12px/700 uppercase `.06em` (`.ncm-war-section__title`); hints: display 9px/500 italic muted (`.ncm-war-section__hint`).

**Admin CSS must use `--ncm-*` vars, not hardcoded brand values, or it won't follow the admin theme.** A 2026-06 sweep converted ~600 solid brand hexes → `var(--ncm-*)` and ~380 brand `rgba(r,g,b,a)` → `color-mix(in srgb, var(--ncm-X) N%, transparent)` across all `admin-panel/tab-*.css` (color-mix is fine in Foundry v12's Chromium). When adding admin-panel styles, follow that discipline. **Intentionally left hardcoded:** network-**type** colors (CorpNet blues etc.), per-preset icon colors, and black/white `rgba` (shadows/overlays — theme-agnostic). Note: much shard-tab CSS reads `var(--sp-X, var(--ncm-Y))` / `var(--ncm-color-X, var(--ncm-X))` — `--sp-*` is undefined outside `.shard-preset-*` scopes and `--ncm-color-*` doesn't exist, so both **fall through to the themed `--ncm-*`**.

## Contacts tab grouping (admin Contacts tab)

The accordion has a **Group-by selector** (the second `<select>` in the search bar, classes `ncm-ct-search__sort ncm-ct-search__group` — the sort listener excludes it via `:not(.ncm-ct-search__group)`): **Role** (default) / Organization / Trust / None (flat "All Contacts"). Choice persists per client in the `contactsGroupBy` setting (lazy-read into `_groupBy` in prepareContext, written on change). Player Characters stays pinned first in EVERY mode. **Role mode** (2026-07-11, dev-picked): one group per role — built-in roles in `roleChipMap` order, then custom/free-text roles alphabetically, **Unsorted** (no role at all) last. Group name = pluralized chip label via the `rolePlural` map (fallback `label + 's'`); an *unrecognized free-typed* role groups by its raw text title-cased (a contact with role "legend" gets a "Legends" group — it does NOT fall into a catch-all). The old hardcoded 4-bucket map (everything unknown → "Street Contacts") is gone — that's what mis-filed custom roles. **Org mode**: orgs alpha, "No Organization" last. **Trust mode**: Implicitly Trusted → Unknown. Collapse keys are slugs of the group name, so remembered collapse state survives.

## Player Contact Manager — last interaction (2026-08-14)

The detail panel's **Last Interaction** card and the **Recent** sort are live (they were TODO placeholders for years). `MessageRepository.getLastMessagesByCounterparty(actorId)` scans the actor's mailbox ONCE and indexes the newest message per counterparty three ways (`byEmail` lowercased / `byActorId` / `byContactId`) — the counterparty is always the OTHER side (sender on received mail, recipient on a `-sent` copy). `MessageRepository.pickLatestFor(index, {email, actorId, masterContactId})` (static) resolves one contact against it, newest across the three keys. `ContactManagerApp._prepareContext` builds the index per render (`_lastMsgIndex`), `_lastMessageFor(contact)` feeds both the `recent` sort (no-history contacts last, A→Z) and the selected contact's card (`{subject, timeAgo}` via the shared `_relativeTime` pattern). Also usable one-off: `getLastMessageWith(actorId, contact)`. Sim coverage in `tools/sims/test-footprints.mjs`.

## Quick Import Actor → contact (admin Contacts tab)

The Contacts-tab toolbar button **Import Actor** (`importActorAsNpc` action → `ContactsTab.onImportActorAsNpc`) opens `ActorImportPickerApp` (`scripts/ui/GMContactManager/ActorImportPickerApp.js`) — a standalone `BaseApplication` listing world actors that are **not** player-owned and **not** already linked to a master contact, with live client-side search (no re-render; rows hidden via `style.display`). Picking a row closes the picker and calls `GMContactManagerApp.startCreateFromActor(actorId)`, which builds a `_createDraft` (name, `actor.img`, NCM email flag, CPR role item → role slug, pre-set `actorId`) and opens the create form pre-filled. The GM form's create branch binds to `createDraft` (not `null`); `_createDraft` is cleared whenever create mode is left (cancel / save / select / new / keyboard-N). Template `templates/gm-contact-manager/actor-import-picker.hbs`; styles `styles/components/actor-import-picker.css`. **Rows hide via the `hidden` attribute + a `[hidden]{display:none !important}` rule** — the row's own `display:flex !important` beats an inline `style.display='none'`, which is why the search "didn't filter" (2026-09 tester report; the JS-side match set was right, so Enter still picked an actor). Enter picks the single match, else the first row whose name starts with the query, else the top visible row.

## Tools tab — Purge Messages (scoped) + Orphan Audit

Admin → Tools → Danger Zone → **Purge Messages** (`purgeMessages` action → `ToolsTab.onPurgeMessages`) is a **scoped** purge, not all-or-nothing. It enumerates targets — character + contact inboxes (via `MessageRepository.identifyInbox`, with per-inbox message counts) and data shards (via the `isDataShard` flag, world + actor-owned, counted by `DataShardService.getShardMessages`) — and presents a grouped `<select>` (plus an "ALL inboxes" option) in a `Dialog`; empty targets are skipped. A confirm dialog shows the exact count, then it deletes. `_purgeInboxJournal(journalId)` deletes only message pages (`flags.<MODULE>.messageId`) so the empty inbox survives; `_purgeShard(uuid)` removes entries via `DataShardService.removeMessage` so the shard item survives. Deletions fire the normal document-sync hooks, so open viewers update. (Read the select via `html.find(...)` in the Dialog callback — `FormDataExtended` is unreliable.)

**Orphan Audit** (`orphanAudit` → `ToolsTab.onOrphanAudit`, 2026-08-14) replaced the two dead Danger-Zone buttons: "Reset Module" (a double-confirm that ended in "not yet implemented") and "Rebuild Index" (called `repository.rebuildIndex()`, **which never existed** — optional chaining swallowed it and it reported success; don't re-add either without a real implementation). The audit scans for NCM data whose owner is gone — inbox journals whose actor/master-contact no longer exists (`_gatherOrphans` via `identifyInbox` + `masterContactService.getContact`), scene pins pointing at deleted terminal items (`flags.<MODULE>.terminalItemId`), and `traceHeat` entries keyed to deleted actors — lists them in a Dialog, and deletes/prunes on confirm.

## Networks tab — Scene Signal list controls

`NetworksTab._gatherSceneStrip()` returns the full enriched scene list (used for the dead-zone stat); `_buildSceneView()` applies search + hide-imported + sort + pagination (8/page, `static SCENE_PAGE_SIZE`) and returns `{ scenes, controls }`. Defaults: sort `current` (current scene first), `_sceneHideImported = true`. "Imported" = compendium-derived, detected via `scene._stats.compendiumSource` (v12) with a `flags.core.sourceId` (v11) fallback; such scenes are hidden **only** when they have no NCM config (`deadZone` / `defaultNetwork` / any truthy `networkAvailability` entry) — configured/jammed scenes always show. The hide button only renders when hideable scenes exist (`controls.canHide = importedCount > 0`), so it can't flip-off-and-vanish. The sort `<select>` and the search input get manual listeners in `onRender` (selects don't fire on change via `data-action`).

## Network Management app — Scenes tab (split-panel + auto-save)

This is the **standalone `NetworkManagementApp`** Scenes tab (`scripts/ui/NetworkManagement/NetworkManagementApp.js` + `templates/network-management/network-management.hbs`), distinct from the AdminPanel Networks tab above. Layout = a **split-panel** mirroring the app's Subnets tab: a fixed summary strip (Scenes / Configured / Dead Zones) on top, then a **searchable, paginated scene list** on the left (`_buildSceneListView()`, 12/page, `static SCENE_PAGE_SIZE`; current scene sorts first; clamps page on shrink) and the **selected scene's config** on the right. The scenes content div carries `ncm-netmgr__content--scenes` (flex column) and the split carries `ncm-netmgr__split--scenes` (`flex:1; height:auto`) so the summary stays pinned and the panes scroll internally. Selection persists across pages/search via `_selectedSceneId`; `_prepareContext` auto-selects current/first when unset or stale.

**Scene config auto-saves on change — there is no Save button.** Default network (`<select.ncm-netmgr__scene-default-select>`) and each availability checkbox (`input.ncm-netmgr__scene-avail-check[data-network-id]`) write the scene flag immediately, matching the dead-zone toggle. Both get **manual `change` listeners in `_onRender`** (selects/checkboxes don't fire reliably via `data-action`); they call the **instance** methods `_saveSceneDefaultNetwork(value)` / `_saveSceneAvailability()` (the latter rebuilds the full availability map from all panel checkboxes so the just-toggled box is included), which call `NetworkService.setSceneDefaultNetwork` / `setSceneNetworkAvailability`. A transient **"✓ Saved"** chip shows via `_flashSavedAndRender()`: it sets `_sceneSavedFlash`, awaits one `render`, then clears the flag so an unrelated later re-render can't replay it (the chip auto-fades via the `ncm-netmgr-saved-flash` CSS keyframes). The pre-redesign bug was that default-network only persisted via a separate Save button buried below an all-scenes chip wall, so it read as "not saving" — the underlying `setFlag` was always correct. The old `saveSceneConfig`/`refreshScenes` actions and the scene-selector/chip-strip markup are gone.

**Network Broadcast goes to the whole table, GMs included — and `whisper: []` is PUBLIC.** `NetworksTab.onSendBroadcast` whispers to every ACTIVE user and makes sure the sender is in the list. It used to filter `!u.isGM`, so the GM who sent it had no record of their own broadcast and the table could not refer back to it; worse, with no players online the list came out empty, and Foundry reads an empty whisper array as a message for everyone — a "broadcast" nobody was meant to see going out in the clear. Including the sender makes the list structurally non-empty, which closes both.

## Architecture: who may author shards (the encoder gate)

**There is ONE answer — `canEncode(actor)` (helpers.js) — and every door reads it.** The Agent's Encode pill (`MessageViewerApp` context flag), the item right-click + convert button + unconvert (`ShardSheetOverride.canAuthor`), the Program Deck's blank cartridge, the `game.nightcity.openEncoder` funnel, and `ShardEncoderApp._permissionProblem()` (which runs on open AND again before the roll, because the operator is switchable from inside the window). A character who does not qualify sees **no button**, never a button that opens and refuses.

It returns `{ok, reason, off}` — `off` distinguishes "the GM has not switched player authoring on" (a world setting) from "this character does not qualify" (a gate the GM wrote), because the fixes differ.

**The gate is `encoderConfig.gate`, byte-for-byte the shape of `DEFAULTS.OPERATOR_GATE`, evaluated by the same `canOperate`** — mode / logic / roles+roleLogic / skill+min / itemGroups (OR-of-ANDs) / actors allowlist / allowGM. No second gating dialect. It is a **separate object** from the terminal operator gate on purpose, so "anyone may operate a terminal, only a Tech may forge a shard" is sayable.
- **`canOperate` gained `{ standalone: true }` for exactly this.** Its normal path merges `DEFAULTS ← the operatorGate SETTING ← override`, so passing the shard gate as a plain override would silently import the terminal's rule for every field the shard gate omits — two gates that look independent in the UI but are not. It also takes `label` (default `'Operator tools require'`); the encoder passes `'Encoding a shard requires'`.
- **Gated with nothing configured is INERT and says so.** `gateSummary()` reports `{gated, inert, parts}` and the GM card prints the live answer ("Right now a character qualifies by: **Tech / Netrunner OR Interface 4+**"). No accidental lockout.
- Sanitising lives in `sanitizeGate` inside the pure `EncoderModel.js` (sim-testable): unknown mode → `anyone`, empty groups and nameless entries **dropped** (an unsatisfiable gate reads as a broken button), rank coerced to a non-negative integer.
- GM card: **Admin → Shards → Player Authoring → "Who can make them"** tab. Role/gear/character choosers are the searchable **`showNamePicker`** dialog, not `<select>`s.

**`ShardConversionFlow` is RETIRED (2026-09-22)** — the file, `templates/dialogs/shard-conversion.hbs`, and both `shard-conversion.css` copies (the admin-panel one was already an orphan, never imported) are deleted, along with `TEMPLATES.SHARD_CONVERSION`. The three settings only it read — **`playerShardFloor`, `playerShardSkill`, `playerBlackICE`** — are no longer registered: a `config: true` setting nothing reads is a knob in Module Settings that silently does nothing. Their Setting documents stay on disk, inert (the namespace-migration precedent). `playerBlackICE` had already been orphaned; the Encoder asks `encoderConfig.lock.blackIce`. The old two-route statics (`encoderEnabled` / `playerAuthoringOn`) are gone with it — one route, one question.

**`scripts/utils/namePicker.js` is THE searchable world picker — every dropdown over a world collection goes through it (2026-10-20).** `showNamePicker({title, hint, entries, allowNone, selected, chips, placeholder, taken})` + `documentEntries(docs, {group:'folder'|'type'|fn, sub, note, fallbackImg})` + `folderPathOf(doc)` + the pure `matchesPicker(entry, q, group)`. Tester: "it's tedious to scroll through hundreds of items… a search, filter or default directory." Rows carry the Foundry **folder path** as a sub-line, **filter chips** are one per top-level folder (or type) with counts, search matches name OR path, Enter picks the first visible row, `allowNone` adds a leading clear row (resolves `{none:true}`), `selected` highlights + scrolls. Routed: Admin → Shards preset Quick Create and Terminals → Import (world items), the composer's three "Link a Scene" chips (ONE `_pickScene` helper), the dossier Linked actor + payload macro (hidden input + read-only label row `.ncm-picker-row`, repainted by `_paintPickerLabels` after edit-prefill), the contact form's Linked Actor (the partial is shared by the player Contact Manager AND the GM Contact Manager — both register `pickLinkedActor` and pass `linkedActorName`), the terminal's Download-to-Agent / Forward targets. **Rule: a `<select>` is for a short enum (skills, networks, folders, players, the handful of ICE actors); anything that lists `game.items/actors/scenes/macros` uses the picker** — `tools/sims/test-world-picker.mjs` (69 checks) greps for a whole-collection `.map(… <option`. It is an `adminDialog` (`.ncm-pkr-dialog`, admin-preset themed, every colour a `--ncm-*` token — the literal greys that were near-white on clean-light are gone; chips are display-font 10px/700 uppercase 2px like the rest of the panel). It had been written four times before this and the copies had drifted. **It filters with inline `style.display`, never the `hidden` attribute** — `[hidden]`'s UA rule loses to the picker row's own `display:flex`, which is why the Encoder config's blank-item search silently did not filter (the ActorImportPicker trap). A chosen entry renders **greyed, not hidden** (`is-taken`), and the click is refused in JS. **Second sweep (same day, dev: "cover all our bases") — eight more surfaces, and the picker is now the ONLY list-of-world-names dialog in the module:** the GM Contact Manager's two **Share-to-character `<select>`s listed EVERY actor in the world** (a template `{{#each}}`, so the JS-only guard never saw it — `_pickShareTarget`, Players chip first then top folders); Tools → **Purge Messages** (a grouped select over every inbox + shard → picker with Everything / Character / Contact / Shard chips, the ALL-inboxes row kept); the **Encoder's key-item picker** (its copy filtered with the `hidden` attribute — the search box never worked); the terminal **burn target** (Carried / World chips, shards tagged first); the terminal-account and both config-sheet (**shard + Network Manager**) key-item pickers; the Security tab's **gate choosers** (whose private `_availableItems` copy silently CAPPED the list at 150 — now `worldItemEntries`/`worldRoleEntries`/`itemImgFor`/`roleImgFor` from the picker); and the network-auth **credential picker** (`scripts/utils/itemPicker.js` is DELETED — literal brand hex in an inline `<style>`, list rebuilt per keystroke; its world variant was already dead code). **`showNamePicker({ admin: false })` for a player-facing caller** (Encoder, terminal viewer, network auth): a plain Dialog whose tokens resolve from `:root` = the player theme; GM tools keep the default admin dialog. `isCarrier(item)` / `NON_CARRIER_TYPES` are exported as the one gear filter. Also fixed on the way: **`NetworkAuthDialog` read `game.user.character` in six places** (a player owning several PCs authenticated and was lockout-tracked as the ASSIGNED one) — it now uses `resolveOperator()` like every viewer. Left as-is because they already search: Admin → Shards → Convert Item (search + per-owner tabs + type filter), View Inbox, Actor Import, the terminal account's Link Identity. The sim guards all of it: no picker-row builder outside namePicker.js, no `.hidden = !!q` filter, no `{{#each …actors}}<option`, itemPicker gone, `admin:false` → no admin class. **Redesign (same day, dev-approved mock `docs/mockups/name-picker-redesign-mock.html`, left = old / right = shipped):** the picker now owns **`styles/components/name-picker.css` under the `ncm-pkr-*` prefix** (dialog class `ncm-pkr-dialog`) — the old `.ncm-burn-dialog` host was a CLASS COLLISION with the Email Setup burn-confirm's content wrapper (email-setup.css forces `* { margin:0 !important }` + literal greys under that class, and it bled into every picker). What changed on screen: sticky **folder headers** in the list while All is active (hidden the moment a chip or query narrows it — the "default directory" without a click), a search **toolbar** with a live shown/total count + clear, chips as **spans** in one scrolling row (a `<button>` in an admin dialog is restyled by admin-theme.css with `!important`), **↑↓ walks a cursor and ⏎ picks it** (Enter with no cursor still picks the first live row), the match highlighted in each name, an empty state that offers "Clear search" / "Search all groups", and a status strip (keys; in multi mode "N selected · select all shown"). The JS contract is unchanged — no caller changed. Tokens only, so admin presets and the player theme (`admin:false`) both drive it. Probe: `docs/mockups/name-picker-probe.html?t=…&mode=multi&q=&cursor=` mounts the REAL `showNamePicker` against the real cascade. Sim: `tools/sims/test-world-picker.mjs`.

Sim: `tools/sims/test-shard-gate.mjs` (34 checks — gate shape, the full `canEncode` matrix, gate independence in BOTH directions, and that the retired wizard left nothing behind).

## Architecture: shard theming

**The shard palette is DATA, not CSS.** Each `SHARD_PRESETS` entry carries an 11-key `palette` (the `COLOR_VAR_MAP` keys); `buildShardThemePrefs(theme, presetKey, {textScale, contrast})` (helpers.js, the twin of `buildTerminalThemePrefs`) layers the shard's own overrides on top; `ItemInboxApp._applyShardTheme` applies it element-scoped through `ThemeService.applyTheme`. The twenty `--sp-*` tokens are **derived from `--ncm-*` in ONE place** (the block at the top of item-inbox.css, shared with `.ncm-data-shard-composer` and `.ncm-malware-workshop`).

This replaced `styles/themes/shard-presets.css` (deleted), which hardcoded all twenty tokens for each of eight presets. Three things that fixed:
1. **The per-shard contrast slider did nothing on 8 of the 9 presets.** Contrast writes `--ncm-*`; a preset's hardcoded `--sp-*` never read them.
2. **`--sp-accent` read `var(--ncm-color-secondary, #19f3f7)` — a variable that does not exist**, so even the blank preset ignored the player's accent and fell through to literal brand cyan (same for `--sp-bar`, `--sp-chip-active-text`).
3. **Typed content could never follow the shard's theme** — the 18 content types render on `--ncm-*` by design.

Verified by `docs/mockups/shard-theme-probe.html` (renders all nine presets through the real ThemeService into the real cascade, diffs every token against the old stylesheet, reports worst drift in `<title>`): **10 of 11 mapped tokens reproduce EXACTLY**; the only drift is `--sp-text-muted`, now a `color-mix` of textMuted toward the accent so one formula reproduces each preset's tinted footer (Δ29–78, same hue family). Small drift for a clearer derivation was the agreed trade.

**`COLOR_VAR_MAP` gained `border` + `textMuted`** — shard presets carry a tinted border and footer as identity. `CONTRAST_SPREAD` had already tone-mapped both under those exact key names, so this closed a half-built contract. No THEME_PRESET defines them, so the player theme is unchanged until someone sets an override, and `variables.css` keeps the `:root` defaults. Adding a key here grows the player Theme Customizer grid and the terminal Theme tab automatically — they all iterate the map.

**Where things are written, and why:**
- The **palette goes on the APP ROOT**, not `.ncm-item-inbox`: the window FRAME sits outside the viewer element and must follow the shard (`.ncm-app.ncm-item-inbox > .window-header` — **compound**, because this window's app root carries both classes while the template root repeats `ncm-item-inbox`; the descendant form is the inner element), and `.ncm-item-inbox` owns `--ncm-text-scale` as a calc of two knobs that `applyTheme` would stomp with a single number. Text scale is passed as the **composed** player×shard product so the header matches the body.
- **Contrast is applied separately, onto the inbox, with `{ baseFrom: appRoot }`** so the tone-map spreads THIS shard's colours (or the player's, when the shard has none) rather than the brand constants `applyTheme`'s own contrast pass falls back to. The viewer therefore passes `contrast: 100` into `buildShardThemePrefs`.
- `config.theme` has **three legal shapes** and the builder tolerates all of them with no migration: the palette object (`{colors, fonts, wallpaper, wallpaperStrength}`), the dead `'classic'`/preset-key **string** `_onSaveConfig` used to write, and the preset **CHROME block** (`accent`/`headerBg`/`watermarkIcon`/`colorTemp`) the Encoder writes — which has no `colors` key, so they coexist.

**Config → Theme tab** (`item-inbox-config.hbs` `data-panel="theme"`, wired by `ItemInboxConfig._wireThemeTab`). Live preview strip, preset cards, an 11-key colour grid with per-key reset, font selects, Text Size + Contrast (moved here from Identity), wallpaper + strength, and the two libraries.
- **The draft contract: theme controls are UNNAMED and data-keyed (`data-stc`), mutate `this._themeDraft`, and NEVER re-render.** `_prepareContext` reads the STORED config, so a `render()` would discard unsaved edits in Identity/Security/Network/Systems/Boot. Everything repaints by hand (`_repaintThemeTab`, `_rebuildSavedThemeList`, `_rebuildFullPresetList`). The one exception is `fullApply`, which writes immediately after a confirm that says so.
- **A cleared override is stored as `''`, never deleted.** `updateConfig` merges through `mergeObject`, which would keep a removed key's old value; `''` is falsy so the builder falls through to the preset exactly as an absent key would.
- The preset **cards drive a hidden `<input name="preset">`** — that is what `_onSaveConfig` and `_onApplyPreset` read.
- Only the borrowed `.ncm-tc__*` widgets are reused; their **button/select resets are root-scoped per host**, so `.ncm-item-inbox-config` had to join those selector lists in theme-customizer.css. Keep them in step.

**Two libraries, and the security rule that separates them:**
- **`shardThemePresets` — saved LOOKS.** Palette, fonts, wallpaper, plus a `basePreset`. Carries **no security block by construction**.
- **`shardPresetLibrary` — full shard presets.** Look + startup + identity, and the **security block ONLY if the GM ticked the box when saving** (unticked by default). An entry that carries one is chipped `SECURITY` on its row and the apply confirm names it. `SHARD_SECURITY_KEYS` is an explicit list, so a config field added later does not silently start travelling inside a preset. Import never auto-applies.
- The reason for all of it: several shipped presets ship Black ICE at DV 20, and reaching for "make another one like this" must never arm a shard without saying so. Same rule as the Encoder never handing `convertToDataShard` a preset KEY.

**Players can wear the GM's looks.** The Encoder's Look row offers the built-in presets and the saved looks (`encoderConfig.look = { presets, savedThemes }`, both default on, each gating its own source). A saved look and a preset are **mutually exclusive picks** — two lit chips claiming to set the same thing reads as a bug. `_lookConfig` layers the saved palette onto the preset chrome (`{...out.theme, ...t}` — no shared keys) and still carries **no security**.


**Optional depth — three real rules (2026-09-22).** `encoderConfig.optional` was four bare booleans that nothing read. It is now `{gear, time, assist}`, each an object with a rule behind it; **`signature` was REMOVED** rather than left as a dead switch (dev: "lets just remove that"). `sanitizeOptional` **lifts a legacy boolean into `{on: <bool>}`** over the defaults, so a world that stored the placeholder shape upgrades with no migration.
- **gear** — `gearBonusFor(cfg, carriedNames)`. Matched by item name the space/punctuation-insensitive SkillService way. `stack: 'best'` (default) counts only the most useful item; `'sum'` adds them, which is how a +9 happens by accident. It returns `from[]` so the readout can NAME what helped — a silent bonus is one the player cannot check, argue with or learn from.
- **time** — `timeCostFor(cfg, dv)` = base + perDV × DV, rendered by `formatMinutes`. Charged whatever the outcome: a rejected forgery still took the afternoon. Advancing the clock is a GM-only write, so `_spendTime()` moves it when it can and **always reports the cost**, whispering the GM when a player cannot move it themselves. Both the clock write and the report are try/caught — running out of permission must never cost a player the shard they just rolled for.
- **assist** — `assistBonusFor(cfg, {helperRank})`. Candidates come from `getPlayerCharacters()`; a helper who fails `requireSkill` is still OFFERED but chipped "no help" and refused on click, so the rule teaches itself instead of leaving the player wondering why the number did not move.

**Every bonus reaches the roll through ONE accessor — `ShardEncoderApp._bonuses()`** (perk + gear + assist). `_base()`, both ladder previews and the roll's `modifier:` all read it. A bonus added at a call site is exactly how the readout and the roll end up quoting different numbers; the sim asserts no preview still adds `_blank().perk` by hand.

**The shard viewer's window title is the shard's own name** — no `DATA SHARD:` prefix (dev, 2026-09-22). It prefers `config.shardName`, then the item name.

**The Player Authoring window's copy is PLAIN, per the standing rule that GM tools use plain language** (in-world vocabulary belongs on player-facing immersive surfaces). Every section subtitle says what the control does, not why it is interesting — the aphoristic first pass read as confusing to the dev. Two lines were also factually stale, claiming the Lock and Content stages were "not built yet" after they shipped; when a stage lands, grep this window for its copy.


Sim: `tools/sims/test-shard-theme.mjs` (45 checks — the palette move, every `buildShardThemePrefs` shape incl. both legacy ones, the viewer wiring, the tab's save contract as a template↔handler drift guard, a REAL Handlebars render of the panel with real data, and the security opt-in).

## Architecture: data shard conversion + entries

Two convert paths, both gated through `ShardSheetOverride._launchConversionFlow(item, actor)` (the right-click "Convert to Data Shard" on actor sheets and the Items directory):
- **GM** converts **instantly with the default config** — `DataShardService.convertToDataShard(item)` (no overrides) — mirroring the Admin → Shards tab "Convert" button. **No wizard.** The GM customizes afterward via the "Configure Shard" context option. (Pre-change the GM also got the player wizard; that was deliberately removed.)
- **Players** get the **Shard Encoder**, gated by `canEncode(actor)` (see the encoder-gate section). It opens on the BLANK stage with the item seeded — deliberately NOT `stage: 'program'`, which used to force every item right-click into the Program branch, so a player who wanted to forge a dossier landed on a page asking which program to build. (The tier-gated `ShardConversionFlow` wizard that used to live here is retired.)

**Admin → Shards → "New Blank Shard" CREATES a fresh world Item** (`ShardsTab._createBlankShard`: CPR `gear` type when offered, name auto-numbered, default icon), converts it with the blank preset, and opens Configure — it used to open the same pick-an-item dialog as the preset quick-create buttons, so it read as a duplicate of "Convert Item" (dev, 2026-09-10). The preset buttons still convert an EXISTING item.

**Shard entries must be written through `DataShardService.addMessage(item, entryData)`** — never by hand-writing a journal page. The reader `DataShardService._pageToShardMessage` (used by `getShardMessages`/`getVisibleEntries`, which the `ItemInboxApp` viewer renders) **discards any page whose flag `type !== 'shard-message'`** and keys on `messageId`/`contentType`. `addMessage` stamps exactly that shape — as does the Encoder, via `programToEntry` / the composer's collected `entryData`. (A prior bug in the retired conversion wizard wrote a raw page with `entryType`/no `messageId` and the entry was silently invisible; that is the failure mode to watch for in any new writer.) **Per-entry `icon`/`accentColor` are NOT stored** — the viewer derives icon/accent from `CONTENT_TYPE_INFO[contentType]`.

**Players can't write to the GM-owned shard journal** (created `OBSERVER`). So the wizard's initial entry **rides along in the conversion socket relay**: `_requestPlayerConversion(item, config, entryData)` → `SHARD_CONVERT_REQUEST` carries `entryData` → the GM handler in `SocketHandlers.js` runs `convertToDataShard` then `addMessage` in one round-trip (entry failure is non-fatal). Eddies debited up-front in the wizard are **refunded on any conversion failure** (`_refundActorWealth`).

**Integrity is opt-in per shard, not a regression.** It renders only when `config.integrity.enabled` is true. `DEFAULTS.SHARD_CONFIG.integrity.enabled` is **`false`** and **no preset enables it** (an Encoder blank tier can, via its `integrity` checkbox), so default-converted shards show no bar until the GM ticks Integrity in Configure Shard → Systems.

**Shard integrity (rebuilt 2026-10-18, tester report "nothing ever looks corrupted") — design + as-built in `docs/SHARD-INTEGRITY.md`.** Three root causes it fixed: cosmetic mode had NO visuals at all (the tier only coloured the bar); mechanical corruption silently failed for players (the page write on the GM-owned journal threw inside a swallowed `.catch`); corruption was all-or-nothing. Standing rules:
- **Two layers.** Shard integrity (one number, the bar) drives the visuals + death at 0. Each entry's `corruption` page flag (0..1, quarter steps; legacy `corrupted:true` reads as 1) drives what is lost (mechanical mode). **Which units are lost is DERIVED, never stored** — `IntegrityModel.isUnitLost` gives each unit a fixed seeded threshold, so loss is monotonic and reconstruct returns the newest losses first.
- **All rules live in the pure `IntegrityModel.js`** (`resolveIntegrity` layers any stored block over `INTEGRITY_DEFAULTS`, 0-safe — no migration); DOM paint in `IntegrityFX.js`. **Lost data is removed BEFORE `enrichContent`** (`applyLoss`), never hidden with CSS — the leak rule.
- **Cosmetic garble is PAINT-OVER**: the real character stays in the DOM (`-webkit-text-fill-color: transparent` + a `::before` glyph), so copy/paste and screen readers get the truth. Capped at 1,200 glyphs per entry. Motion is vetoed by OS reduced-motion or the animation pref; the garble stays.
- **Every integrity write is GM-side; players relay** via `SOCKET_OPS.SHARD_INTEGRITY_REQUEST` (ops degrade/entry/repair/salvage). `degradeIntegrity` writes **pages first, then the item** — the viewer re-renders off the item update. The threshold is a **percent** (it used to compare raw points). **The config save carries `currentIntegrity`/`history`/`salvaged` over** (`_collectIntegrity`) — it used to reset to Max, healing the shard on every save.
- Protected money (eddies/banking never lose eb), programs turn **unstable** (`programInstability` → `unstable` on `runPayloadExecute`/`runMalwareDeploy`: +DV and a misfire chance) — both GM toggles. Uploads bake the damage into the copy (`exportEntry`; a fully lost entry can't be uploaded).
- The ShardsTab **Integrity** box has a Damage button (+ `game.nightcity.messenger.damageShard`, GM macro "Damage Selected Shard") and an **Entry damage** list; the network access-mode picker's card-clearing is now scoped to its own group because the Integrity section reuses `.ncm-cfg-am-card`.
- **The index strip never says more than the cards** (`_applyIntegrityToChips`): chips take their title from the ENRICHED entry (garbled at 75%+ for players, "Sector lost" when gone — it used to print the real stored subject), carry a 4-segment damage bar, and give the GM the exact % as a tooltip. Chip titles get the paint-over garble at half rate from Unstable down; the chip itself is a `data-action`, so its title is garbled with a narrower skip list than card bodies.
- Sim `tools/sims/test-integrity.mjs` (40); probe `docs/mockups/shard-integrity-probe.html` (real viewer code); design mock `shard-integrity-mock.html`. **Test setup:** `tools/test-integrity-setup.macro.js` (GM Script macro) builds four shards on a carrier actor — Breach Me / Pre-damaged / Visual Only / Bricked — and lists what to try.

**Shard text size (2026-09-10, tester "old eyes") — two knobs, multiplied.** `item-inbox.css` and the shared `content-types.css` had **zero** `--ncm-text-scale` plumbing (226 + 218 fixed-px font-sizes), so the player's Theme Customizer Text Size did nothing in the shard viewer and only partly in typed content anywhere. Both are now fully swept to `calc(Npx * var(--ncm-text-scale, 1))` (mechanical; the sweep skips `font-size: 0` and comments). On top of that a **per-shard Text Size slider** (80–150, step 5) sits in the shard config's Preset & Theme section → `config.textScale` (default 100, `DEFAULTS.SHARD_CONFIG`). The composition is pure CSS: `ThemeService.applyTheme` publishes the player scale on `:root` a second time as **`--ncm-text-scale-base`** (root only — a custom property can't read its own inherited value), the viewer template stamps `--ncm-shard-text-scale` inline on `.ncm-item-inbox` (from `shardTextScale` in `_prepareContext`), and the item-inbox.css root block defines `--ncm-text-scale: calc(base × shard)`. So a player at 130% reading a shard the GM set to 120% gets 1.56×, live from either slider. The config slider previews DOM-only onto every open viewer of that item (`_previewShardTextScale` sets the var, no render) and `close()` reverts an unsaved drag. **Admin-tagged windows now force `--ncm-text-scale: 1`** (admin-theme.css root rule): GM tools size text with the admin *zoom*, and before this the player scale stacked on top of it for every calc'd surface inside a tagged window (GM-view viewer/composer, the entry composer's live preview — and, post-sweep, the entry composer's own form). Terminals are untouched: they set `--ncm-text-scale` on their own root and never read the base. **`malware-workshop.css` is swept too (2026-09-12, all 77 sizes)** — inert inside the GM Workshop (admin-tagged → scale forced to 1, the admin zoom sizes it) but required because the player-facing Shard Encoder (designed, not built — `docs/mockups/shard-encoder-fui-mock.html`) is hosted in the Workshop frame and reuses its `.ncm-fw-*` classes; the dev's rule is that the Workshop's type tiers are the Encoder's model. The FUI modals (security-console / upload-console / bank-heist / operator-select) still have no scale plumbing. Verified in headless Chrome against the real core+CPR+module cascade (shard 1.2×1.5 → 23.4px from 13px; default stamp → player scale only; admin-tagged → 13px; terminal → its own 1.1×).

**Shard contrast (same day) — a per-shard Contrast slider (70–140) beside Text Size → `config.contrast`.** Same OKLCH tone-map as the Theme Customizer (`adjustLightness` around `CONTRAST_SPREAD.pivot`, accents excluded, images untouched), packaged as **`applyContrastToElement(el, pct, {baseFrom})`** (helpers.js): for each `CONTRAST_SPREAD` token it reads the base colour from **`:root`'s computed value** (i.e. the player's own already-contrast-adjusted palette, via `cssColorToHex`; the constants' hex when :root has nothing), pushes it from the pivot, and writes it **inline on the viewer root** — where the shard's `--sp-*` aliases (which read `--ncm-*` on the SAME element) and the shared content-types CSS pick it up. A slider drag or re-render can't compound, because the base is always re-read rather than accumulated; **100% removes the inline overrides** so an untouched shard keeps following the player theme live. Applied in `ItemInboxApp._applyShardTheme` right after the preset class (`shardContrast` in context); the config previews live on open viewers through the same helper and `close()` reverts an unsaved drag (`_displayPreviewed`/`_displaySaved` cover both sliders). Terminals already had contrast through `buildTerminalThemePrefs` → `ThemeService.applyTheme` and are untouched.

> **CORRECTED 2026-09-22.** This paragraph used to claim "shard presets never override the `--sp-*` palette in CSS … presets only carry accent/headerBg/icon". **That was wrong**, and the error was load-bearing: `styles/themes/shard-presets.css` hardcoded all **twenty** `--sp-*` tokens for each of the eight themed presets, and since the contrast tone-map writes `--ncm-*`, those presets' `--sp-*` never read it — **the contrast slider silently did nothing on 8 of the 9 presets.** The fix is the palette move described in the Shard theming section: presets now carry a JS `palette`, the `--sp-*` tokens derive from `--ncm-*` in ONE place (item-inbox.css), and `applyContrastToElement` is called with `{ baseFrom: <app root> }` so it tone-maps *this shard's* colours rather than the player's. The `baseFrom`-less form still reads `:root`, which is what the Encoder and other callers want.

**Index strip open/close animation.** The viewer's Index nav chips cascade in/out via CSS keyframes staggered by a per-chip `--ncm-chip-i` custom property (set in `item-inbox.hbs`). `_onToggleIndexStrip` adds `--anim` (flow-in, forced reflow to restart) on open and `--closing` (flow-out) on close, then a JS timer (`220 + chipCount×25`ms) adds `--collapsed` (`display:none`) once the out-animation finishes. Rapid toggles resolve via an "is collapsed OR closing" check + `clearTimeout`; `prefers-reduced-motion` collapses instantly.

## Architecture: the ENVELOPE — mail vs file (#37)

Every entry used to wear an **email envelope** (from / subject / received) even when it was a camera feed. A terminal is a **machine**, and machines hold **files**. So an entry now carries **`envelope: 'mail' | 'file'`** (default `'mail'` — **every legacy entry is untouched, zero migration**).

- **`file`** = a machine artifact: **filename / path / owner-node / modified / size** instead of a sender. It also **suppresses every mail affordance**: no avatar, no Reply, no Add-to-contacts, **no threading**, **no delivery toast**, and **no master-contact auto-create** (a file's owner is a NODE — `SECCAM-B2`, `root`, `kowalski` — not an Agent).
- **`resolveEnvelope(entry)`** (ContentRenderers, the one source of truth for all three viewers + the composer) returns the file view-model, **deriving everything the GM didn't author**: `deriveFileSize` (12 frames → ~14 MB; a **live feed derives NO size — it's a stream, not a file**), `suggestFilename` (subject → slug + per-type extension via `FILE_TYPE_INFO`; a feed gets `.feed`, not `.img`), and the **type-aware 4th cell** (Channels / Rows / Lines / Subjects / Frames…). A live feed also **renames itself**: `typeLabel` is **CAMERA FEED**, not "Image Gallery" — the mode is its identity — with the broadcast icon and a green accent.
- **`isStream`** is load-bearing: a stream has **no size and no modified date ("LIVE")**, and it cannot be copied off the machine (see exfil).
- **Composer**: a **Show as MESSAGE / FILE** segmented control at the top of ② DELIVERY swaps the section's fields (DOM-only, so a flip-flop doesn't lose unsaved edits). `FILE_DEFAULT_TYPES` pre-picks FILE for the obviously-file types (feed gallery / syslog / malware / schematic / payload / spreadsheet / record / credentials / chatlog / avlog / document) — **a default, not a rule**; one click flips it.
- **Persistence — FOUR field-by-field spots, all four must carry it**: `TerminalService._buildMessageFlags`, `MessageRepository.createMessage`, `MessageRepository._pageToMessage`, **and `DataShardService.addMessage` + `_pageToShardMessage`** (a shard holds files too, and a burned file must stay a file). The edit-prefill openers (`TerminalViewerApp._onEditMessage`, `ItemInboxApp._onEditEntry`) must ALSO carry `envelope`/`file`/`exfil` — the edit path writes `entryData` straight to flags, so a missing key silently resets a FILE back to mail on the next save.
- **Page name**: a file titles its journal page `[FILE] <filename>` — the mail formatter would call it "Unknown".
- **Threading**: `resolveThreads` skips `envelope: 'file'` outright. Signal 4 (subject + shared counterparty) would otherwise merge two unrelated `dock-cam.feed` files from the same node into one "conversation".
- **AUDIT (same round)** — two more silently-dropped fields found in `_buildMessageFlags` and fixed: **`status.eddiesClaimed`/`eddiesClaimedAt`** (hardcoded false → a mirrored/exfil copy of an already-claimed eddies drop was **re-claimable**) and **`selfDestruct`** (hardcoded null). Same class of bug as the `spam`/`folder`/`recovered*` drops. **When adding a message field, grep all four builders.**

**Per-entry exfil override (#37).** `entry.exfil = { mode: 'inherit'|'custom', <action>: { state: 'inherit'|'allow'|'deny', dv? }, denyReason, hideDenied }`, resolved by **`TerminalService.resolveEntryExfil(item, entry)`** layered over `getExfilPolicy`. Rules:
- **`mode` is the control people expect and it MUST exist** (it was in the mock, got dropped in the first build, and its absence made the whole feature read as broken). `'inherit'` (default) = an untouched action follows the terminal's Exfil tab — so "set one action to ALLOW and hide the rest" changes **nothing visible**, because the rest are still inheriting an *allow*. **`'custom'`** = the entry is the whole story: anything not explicitly ALLOWed is **denied**. `custom` + `hideDenied` is the "allow only this one, lock everything else away" intent. The composer surfaces it as a **FOLLOW TERMINAL / ONLY WHAT I ALLOW** toggle with a live note explaining what INH means in the current mode, and `resolveEntryExfil` emits a `log.debug` of the resolved per-action outcome (debugMode) so "the override isn't doing anything" is diagnosable.
- **The terminal's `allowExfil` master is a HARD CEILING** — master off = nothing leaves, and no entry can widen it.
- **A live camera feed can never be copied** (`agent`/`shard`/`forward`/`sell`/`leak` are force-denied with reason **LIVE STREAM**, regardless of authoring — *you cannot download a stream*). **WIPE still works** (you can always pull the plug) and **SAVE FRAME is the escape hatch**. This is physics, so it's enforced in the resolver, not left to the GM to remember.
- **A denied action renders DEAD WITH ITS REASON, it does not vanish** (`.is-denied` + `.ncm-term__exfil-why`) — a missing button reads as a broken UI, a stated reason teaches the rule. `hideDenied` forces the silent hide for things whose existence is the secret.
- Composer UI: a per-action **INHERIT / ALLOW / DENY** tri-state grid in ③ SECURITY & ACCESS (it's an access policy, not envelope metadata), with per-action DV, denied-reason and hide-instead.

**SAVE FRAME (`TerminalService.exfilSaveFrame`).** You can't take the stream — you can take a **picture** of it. One channel's frame becomes a **single-image gallery** in the operator's Agent (Recovered), stamped with the cam id + the feed clock, carrying a **file envelope**. A **looped** cam captures its *frozen* time (loop a cam then save a frame and you've captured the loop — the correct, cruel outcome). GM writes directly; **players relay** via `TERMINAL_EXFIL_REQUEST` `dest: 'saveFrame'`. Deliberate simplification: the capture is the frame's **source image**, not a pixel-crop of the PTZ view — cropping would mean rasterizing to a data URI and stuffing hundreds of KB of base64 into world data on every capture; instead the **PTZ aim (pan/tilt/zoom) rides in the caption**. The lightbox button only renders when the host stamps `data-save-frame` on the thumb (`TerminalViewerApp._stampFrameCapture`) — ContentRenderers stays policy-free because it serves all three viewers.

**Sidebar (terminal)**: one **mixed list**, and **both row kinds share ONE hierarchy** — this is the rule, don't drift from it:
- **line 1 = the ANCHOR** (sender name / filename) — same size + weight in both, `text-primary`; mail uses the body font, a file uses mono (it's a machine name, not a person's). The priority dot ↔ the **content-type icon** swap here.
- **line 2 = what it is** — the subject, or a small **type chip** (+ size). Typed *mail* gets the same chip, so a spreadsheet reads the same whether it arrived as mail or sits there as a file.
- **line 3 = muted detail** — the body snippet, or the file's path.

The first cut anchored mail on a **14px bold subject** while a file anchored on its **11.5px filename**, so a mixed list read as **two competing lists** (dev: "the actual list of messages/files are confusing"). Unread files show **NEW** (a file has no urgency — it just wasn't there before); a live feed's date column reads **LIVE**. **ALL / MAIL / FILES** solo chips ride the gallery chip driver (`[data-env-act]`, document-delegated) and only render when the folder holds both kinds.

**The exfil folder's NAME is a setting, and every surface reads it.** The Agent folder that catches exfiltrated intel (downloaded mail, burned files, captured camera frames) defaults to **EXFIL**, not "Recovered". Two reasons the old name broke down: the player clicks **EXFILTRATE → To my Agent** and it landed in a folder with a *different* word (two vocabularies for one idea), and "recovered" implies something was lost and found — a **SAVE FRAME capture of a live feed was never lost**, it wasn't even a file until you made one. The label is the world setting **`exfilFolderName`** (GM-renameable — Salvage / Cache / Recovered / The Haul…), read through **`SettingsManager.getExfilFolderName()`**. **The folder tab, the per-message provenance chip, and every exfil toast (including the socket-relay ones) all read that one getter** — hardcoding the word in any of them makes them disagree the moment a GM renames it. The stored flag key stays `metadata.recovered` and the filter key stays `'recovered'`, so renaming is a pure label change with no data migration.

**Terminal motion (#38) — the terminal inherits the MessageViewer's motion vocabulary, it does not invent its own:** `cubic-bezier(.22,1,.36,1)` (expo-out), **0.3s** entrances / **0.18s** micro-interactions, **translate + fade only** (never scale-only, never bounce), all `prefers-reduced-motion` gated. Tokens live on `.ncm-terminal-viewer` (`--ncm-term-ease/fast/base`). What moves: message rows **cascade in** (`--ncm-i` stagger, capped at 9 so a full page can't become a wait), the **detail pane slides** when a different message is opened, the **sidebar collapse animates the grid column**, the **account-switcher dropdown unfolds** from its top edge, and the **login pick** holds the chosen card while the others fall away (`_playCardPick` — cards/roster/portraits; crt/lockscreen/keypad opt out by having no wrapper).

**A CSS transition cannot survive an AppV2 re-render — collapse/expand toggles must be DOM-ONLY.** `render()` **replaces** the element, so it is born in its final state and the transition has nothing to animate *from*: the panel snaps. Both collapse toggles (`TerminalViewerApp._onToggleSide`, `MessageViewerApp._toggleListPanel`) therefore flip the class on the **live node** and update the chevron by hand, and the template still stamps the class from state so it survives the next real render. This is why the terminal's collapse "wasn't working" — it was rendering, not transitioning. Same rule applies to any future animated toggle.

**The MessageViewer runs the same motion system** (row cascade, detail slide, animated list-panel collapse, refine-bar hover) — `MessageViewerApp._stampMotion` is the twin of the terminal's, with the list signature keyed on actor · filter · sort · page · search · env-solo · tab · message ids.

**THE trap, and the reason `_stampMotion` exists: the terminal re-renders on EVERY click.** An entrance animation tied to "a render happened" would make the list twitch every time you select a message. So `_stampMotion` compares a **signature of the list's CONTENTS** (account · folder · env-solo · page · message ids — deliberately **excluding the selection**) against the last render and only stamps `is-fresh` when the list actually changed; the detail's `is-fresh` keys on the selected id changing, so re-rendering the *same* message (claiming eddies, decrypting a frame) doesn't re-slide it. Rule: **motion on arrival and on state change, never on idle.** Sim: scratchpad `test-motion.mjs` (12/12, drives the real `_stampMotion`).

**Terminal sidebar collapses (`terminalToggleSide` → `_sideCollapsed`).** The mailbox folds to nothing so a spreadsheet / camera multiplex / schematic gets the machine's full width (the MessageViewer `--list-collapsed` precedent). Two details that matter: the pane is **`display:none`d, not just width:0** (its children carry min-widths and would spill into the detail pane), and the **toggle floats on the divider, outside the collapsing pane** — a toggle that collapses along with its panel can't bring it back.

**Filter chips that live in a re-rendering app must be APP STATE, not a CSS class.** The MAIL/FILES chips first shipped as the gallery's display-only `[data-*-act]` driver — but the message viewer and terminal **re-render on every click**, so the filter reset itself the instant you opened an item. They're now real `data-action`s backed by `_envFilter`, and the filter **narrows the message list array** rather than hiding rows with CSS (hiding leaves pagination counting things you can't see, and counts computed off the paginated *page* make the chips lie on page 2). Counts are taken from the whole folder BEFORE the solo applies; the solo is force-cleared when the folder isn't mixed, so a stale solo can't empty a folder that has no chips left to undo it. The gallery's chips stay display-only — correct there, since a gallery re-render is scoped to its own entry.

**Modal layer order (learned the hard way).** `.ncm-seccon-backdrop` (SecurityConsole) is **z-index 130**, above the gallery lightbox's **110** — every roll launched from the MONITOR rail (SAVE FRAME, LOOP, CUT, TAKE CONTROL) opens **while the lightbox is still on screen**, and at its old z-index 100 the console rendered *underneath* it ("save frame appears underneath the gallery pop up"). The console is the thing being interacted with, so it's always topmost; the lightbox deliberately stays open behind it (you see the frame you're capturing) and the flow closes it afterward. BankHeist (60) / OperatorSelect (90) never open over a lightbox, so they sit below it.

**The ACCESS-GRANTED badge scan rides `--ncm-primary`, not `--ncm-secondary`.** The secondary slot stays brand cyan in most presets, so on Arasaka (red primary) the verification scan swept **blue across a red card** and read as a foreign element. Primary = the machine's identity colour, so the scan always belongs to the card it's scanning. (Same reason the welcome line under ACCESS GRANTED uses `:has(+ .ncm-term__badge-welcome)` to drop the granted line's bottom padding — it stacked with the welcome's top margin into a dead 24px band.)

**Terminal chip/button sizing.** The sender-header chips (Add to contacts · Reply via Agent) and the whole **exfil rail** (`.ncm-term__btn`) are deliberately **small** (9.5px mono, 3–4px padding, 4px radius) — they were 11–12px with 6–9px padding and dominated the detail pane. There can be **seven** exfil chips at once (six actions + SAVE FRAME, several of them dead deny-chips), so they must stay chip-sized; the GM edit/delete/move controls match at 24px.

## Architecture: paste hygiene + Import from Journal (2026-10-23)

Tester: pasting a journal page into a shard **Document** gave black text on the dark theme and white blocks, typing afterwards stayed black, the light theme was no better, and "one paragraph per box" made bringing journals over impractical (PDF copies arrive hard-wrapped on top). **Root cause: a browser's default paste writes the SOURCE's computed styles inline** (a journal is `#191813` on parchment → every span lands with its own `color`/`background-color`), and a container's `color … !important` can never beat a child's inline declaration; the block was saved as raw `innerHTML` and rendered raw, so players got it too. Typing "stayed black" because the caret was inside the pasted span.

- **`scripts/utils/richText.js` is the ONE pure module** (no Foundry/DOM; sim `tools/sims/test-rich-paste.mjs`, 96 checks). `cleanHtml(html, {mode, colors})` is a tokenizer + tolerant tree whitelist cleaner. Modes: `inline` (a list item/caption), `block` (a Document text block: inline + `p`/`br`/lists; `div`→`p`, headings→bold line, blockquote/pre→p, img/hr/tables dropped), `rich`/`mail` (a message body: + headings/blockquote/pre/hr/img/tables). **Colour policy: a colour survives ONLY if it is the module's** — a `var(--ncm-*)` value or a hex in `RICH_TEXT_PALETTE` (the shard RTE's swatches; `RTE_COLORS` now aliases it). At PASTE time even palette colours are dropped (`colors:'none'`). Also kept: `font-weight/style`, `text-decoration`, margins/paddings, relative `font-size` ≤ 2em, `ncm-*` classes, `data-label`, safe `href`/`src` (no `javascript:`/`data:`). The Google Docs `<b style="font-weight:normal">` wrapper is unwrapped, Word's `o:p`/comments dropped. **Block contract: a one-paragraph block is bare inline HTML, a multi-paragraph block is a `<p>…</p>` sequence** (Enter in the editor writes `<div>`s → normalised to `<p>` at save).
- **Three layers, never just one:** PASTE (the editor's `paste` listener: shard Document blocks via a delegated listener on `.ncm-docpage` → `_onDocPaste`; the shard message RTE → `_onRtePaste`; the Agent mail editor in `MessageComposerApp._setupEditorSync`), SAVE (`DataShardComposer._collectDocRow` cleans `block`, `_collectEntryData` cleans the body `rich`), RENDER (`ContentRenderers` document enricher cleans text `block` + list items `inline`; a plain message body `rich`; `MessageViewerApp._renderMessageBody` cleans `mail` BEFORE the encrypted-block regex — attribute order is preserved so it still matches; `TerminalViewerApp._sanitizeBody` now routes through it, so a bold line/list no longer flattens to plain text on the machine). Render-time cleaning heals shards and mail saved before this shipped with no migration. **A new contenteditable surface = a paste listener + a save clean + a render clean**; the sim counts them.
- **Paste → blocks (`pasteToBlocks` / `htmlToBlocks`)**: headings start a section and become the heading of whatever follows (text, a list, a figure); consecutive paragraphs stay in ONE text block (a block is a SECTION, not a sentence); lists/figures/quotes are their own blocks; a table becomes one paragraph of rows; `@UUID[…]{Label}` enrichers flatten to the label (`enrichers:true`, the journal-import path). **Plain text (a PDF viewer puts only text on the clipboard) is REFLOWED** (`reflowText`): hard-wrapped lines rejoin (a non-sentence-ending line ≥55% of the paragraph's longest line, or a lowercase successor), `word-` + lowercase dehyphenates, ligature glyphs (ﬁ ﬂ ﬀ) expand, NBSP/soft-hyphens normalise, bullet runs become list blocks, a block whose longest line is under 40 chars keeps every break (an address, verse), and a bare page number (`12`, `Page 3 of 9`) is dropped only when the paste has ≥3 paragraphs. The reflow GUESSES, so **an after-paste strip (`.ncm-docpaste`) offers Undo and, when several paragraphs landed in one block, "Split into N blocks"**; a per-block `docSplitBlock` tool (`has-paras` class, shown only where it applies) does the same later. Undo rebuilds the list from a `_collectDocBlocks()` snapshot — the native undo stack cannot restore blocks that live outside the contenteditable.
- **Import from Journal** (`docImportJournal`, the Body fieldset's actions row): the shared name picker over every journal TEXT page (sub-line = journal · folder path, chip = top folder), `fromUuid` → `page.text.content` → `htmlToBlocks(…, {enrichers:true})`; Replace/Append dialog when blocks already exist; an empty Title takes the page name.
- **Viewer/editor fixes that rode along:** the text block renders as `<div class="ncm-ct-doc__p">` (a `<p>` cannot hold paragraphs; CSS + the declassified tint cover both); `.ncm-docpage` background is `--sp-bg-deep` (was a literal `#14141c` — near-black under clean-light); the editor's heading input wears the viewer's accent mono look (it was bright display text, so "white while typing, orange displayed" read as a bug); `.ncm-docpara__t *` inherits colour/background so a legacy pasted block reads correctly in the editor before its first save; the Agent viewer gained rules for h1–h6/blockquote/code/pre/hr/ul/ol/img/table in mail bodies (they rendered in Foundry/CPR defaults); the Agent composer's monospace tool writes a bare `<code>` (its inline literal hex ignored the theme). List items used to render raw `{{{this}}}` with no escaping (a typed `<3` broke the list) — cleaned `inline` now.
- Probe: `docs/mockups/doc-paste-probe.html?t=clean-dark|clean-light|high-contrast&raw=1&strip=1&pdf=1` lifts the REAL `_docBlockHtml` out of the composer source (CRLF-safe regex — the repo is CRLF on Windows), renders the real `content-body.hbs`, and reports worst text contrast in `<title>`: the raw paste measures **1.06:1** (the tester's screen), cleaned **8.81:1** dark / **6.79:1** light. **Not live-tested in Foundry.**

## Architecture: shared content renderers (shards + terminals)

Typed content (a SPREADSHEET / CALENDAR / SYSLOG / GALLERY / … vs. a plain message) renders through **one shared registry** so a file looks identical wherever it's opened — design doc §8c, §9b. The two halves live in `scripts/ui/content/ContentRenderers.js` + `templates/content/content-body.hbs` (`TEMPLATES.CONTENT_BODY`) and **must stay in lock-step**:

- **`CONTENT_TYPE_INFO`** — the single source of truth for per-type `{ label, icon, accent, indexIcon }`. **Moved here from `ItemInboxApp`** (it was a module-local const); the shard viewer now imports it. `ACCENT_HEX` (palette key → hex for chips/dots) lives here too.
- **`enrichContent(entry, { isGM, interactive })`** — turns a raw entry (`{ contentType, contentData, subject, from, body, timestamp, encrypted, decrypted, … }`) into the view-model the partial renders: `is<Type>` flags (generated from `CONTENT_TYPES`, so `avlog`→`isAvlog`), `formattedDate`, `contentTypeLabel`/`entryIcon`/`accentColor`, `isLocked` (`encrypted && !decrypted`), and per-type processed `contentData` (eddies/dossier/payload/avlog enrichers in `ENRICHERS`). `isGM`/`interactive` ride **on the enriched object** (not `../`) so the partial reads them directly.
- **`content-body.hbs`** renders ONLY the decrypted content body (the `{{#if isMessage}}…{{else if isEddies}}…` chain + fallback). The **caller owns** the corrupted/encrypted overlays and the entry header. Interactive affordances (claim/execute/goToScene) are gated on `interactive` — the shard passes `interactive:true`, read-only consumers pass `false`.

**Adding a content type = four touches, nothing else:** one `CONTENT_TYPES` entry · one `CONTENT_TYPE_INFO` row · (optional) one `ENRICHERS` fn · one `{{else if isX}}` branch in `content-body.hbs`. (CSS for a new type goes in `styles/components/content-types.css`, `.ncm-app`-scoped, **only `--ncm-*` tokens** so it renders in both viewers — never bare `--sp-*`.) A fifth touch to make it **authorable**: a `{{#if isX}}` fieldset + a `_collectX(data)` case in `DataShardComposer` (below).
