- **The ladder is the upload arc's, not a second one.** `SLOPPY_BAND` mirrors `FORGE_SLOPPY_BAND` in `UploadFlow.js` and **both the sim and Phase 6 assert they match** — meet the DV = clean · miss by up to the band = sloppy (it writes, with a tell) · worse = rejected (nothing compiles). `encodeOutcome` / `ladderOdds` / `likelyRung` / `oddsClass`. Quality uses the same `50 + margin × 8` scale, so a player-made file reads to ANALYZE/SWEEP exactly like an uploaded forgery.
- **A chip can carry SEVERAL files, and a crowded chip is a harder chip.** `S.entries[]` holds them; `contentStackDV()` prices the lot: the **hardest** file sets the base (you are only as good as the fake that takes the most work), the craft-skill penalty is paid **once**, and every file past the first adds `cfg.content.extraEntryDV` (default 2, 0 turns it off). One encode roll covers the chip, so **one quality lands on every file**. How many fit at all is the tier's `capacity`. Corollaries the code enforces: `_needsRoll()` reads EVERY file (a note beside a forged dossier does not make it easier), `_eddiesLoad()` sums every cred drop on the chip, `_choices()` offers the trades for the HARDEST type, and the GM relay accepts a LIST — a player sending three files must not lose two.
- **ADDING a file and EDITING one are different doors, and the add door needs BOTH halves.** "Write another file" only sets the intent (`S.adding`) and opens the type row; **picking the type is what opens the editor** (`case 'type'` consumes the flag). The first cut stopped at the rail, where the button re-read as "Write another file" again — so the editor was unreachable for a second file and the only way in was the per-file pencil, which EDITS: every "new" file replaced the last one. Any unrelated click clears `adding`, so a later type pick can never surprise-open the editor. The slot a composer writes back to is **captured when it opens**, never read from the live `editIndex` at save time (a second composer would otherwise land its file on top of another one), and a second composer is refused outright — `PlayerEntryComposer` owns ONE window id, so two would fight over the same slot.
- **The startup wears the chip the player designed.** `_bootConfig` sets `iconMode:'image'` + `imageUrl` from the chosen art (a baked data URI works — the boot renders a plain `<img>`), falling back to a glyph only when there is no art. Without it the splash animated around an empty space.

- **A tier says how much the chip HOLDS.** `capacity` (entries; **0 = no limit**) is the fourth thing a tier buys, alongside price, botch rule and perk — defaults Scrap 1 / Standard 3 / Premium 8, so a better blank is worth buying for more than surviving a botch. `capacityOf()` / `capacityLeft()` are the readers. The encode stamps the tier's limit onto the shard as `config.capacity`, and **`DataShardService.addMessage` enforces it for every writer** — the Encoder, the GM composer, the Workshop, and a terminal burn — returning `{success:false, full:true}` with a sentence rather than failing silently. **An absent capacity resolves to 0/unlimited**, so every shard authored before this behaves exactly as it did. A burn onto a full chip now carries the reason out of `exfilToShard` (it counted successes and returned a bare `success:false`, which read as a broken button).

- **Blank tiers are GM-defined.** `tierForItem(cfg, {name, tierId})` takes a PLAIN DESCRIPTOR, never a Foundry Item (the model stays pure — the app's `_desc(item)` builds it). An explicit `tierId` stamped on a bought blank wins; else the GM's per-tier item lists by **space/punctuation-insensitive name** (the SkillService rule); else the fallback tier. `botchFor(tier, {nat1})` says what a rejection costs — a natural 1 is one step harsher. A tier perk (`craft+1`) is a **real modifier**: `SkillService.performCheck` gained an `options.modifier` that lands in the total and the result.
- **Program branch:** `FAMILY_ABILITIES` maps utility/execute → `PROGRAM_EFFECTS` keys. **Only verbs with a working applier are offered** — `wipe`/`corrupt` need target files picked at PUSH time and `logicBomb` has no inner-effect authoring, so all three are absent (the standing "a dead option is worse than no option" rule; the sim asserts the negatives). Rank in the gating skill both **unlocks** abilities and **buys charges**; the player then PICKS the craft skill (`craftChoices` — the recommended one free, another trade at +DV, some alts limited to certain verbs). `programDV` = ability DV + craft penalty + trojan cover. `programEfficiency(total, dv, rank)` is what the margin buys: clean = full charges / stealth DV 12+margin (capped 20) / run DV −2; sloppy = 1 charge / stealth DV 8 / run DV +2 / GLITCHY.

**The Content branch does NOT reimplement authoring — it opens the REAL composer.** `PlayerEntryComposer` (scripts/ui/ShardEncoder/) subclasses `DataShardComposer` for exactly two reasons a per-instance option cannot cover: `ADMIN_THEMED` is read as `this.constructor.ADMIN_THEMED` and this window is player-facing, and it needs its own window id so it can't collide with a composer the GM has open. The parent gained a **`playerMode`**: it **COLLECTS instead of persisting** (`onCollect(entryData)` — the Encoder keeps the entry, and only the encode roll decides whether it is ever written), its `_prepareContext` / `_onSaveEntry` isGM gates relax, the type picker is filtered to the GM's `allowedTypes`, and the template hides the GM-only apparatus (③ SECURITY & ACCESS, the template library) behind `{{#unless playerMode}}`. The inherited static actions still work because they are bound to the PARENT's methods by name and branch on the flag.
- **The roll is the forgery's quality, not a gate.** `contentDV` (the type's DV + the penalty for using the other trade) → `contentEfficiency` → `quality` on the same `50 + margin × 8` scale, and a **`scanDV` that mirrors `UploadFlow.stealthDVFromQuality` exactly** (the sim asserts the formula still matches), written to the entry as **`contentData.sniffDV`** — which is what ANALYZE rolls against. So a shard a player forged reads to ANALYZE and SWEEP like one they uploaded, and a sloppy fake really is easier to catch.
- **A type with no craft skill needs no roll at all** (`_needsRoll()`): a message, a note and a cred drop are not fakes, so the die never appears, the ladder is replaced with "nothing to fake, nothing to roll", and the blank is never at risk. Do not "fix" this by giving them a DV.
- The centre panel previews the finished file through `enrichContent` + the shared `content-body` partial, with the `--sp-*` tokens mapped onto the preview so a typed file looks the way it will on the chip.

**The LOOK is the player's, the SECURITY is the roll's.** The Blank stage's **Look** row sets the chip art and a cosmetic preset. Art defaults to the Cyberpunk RED compendium chip (`systems/cyberpunk-red-core/icons/compendium/gear/memory_chip.svg`, `DEFAULT_SHARD_IMG`) with five one-click alternatives from the same set and a FilePicker for anything else; it is written to the **item**, never into the shard config (`_lookConfig` stamps `__img` and `_shardConfig` strips it). A preset contributes **`theme` + `boot` ONLY** — several shipped `SHARD_PRESETS` carry Black ICE at DV 20, and a player must not inherit that by liking the artwork, so the Encoder passes explicit overrides and never hands `convertToDataShard` a preset KEY (which would apply `security` too). Security comes from the Lock stage, where it is rolled for.

**The roll follows what was WRITTEN, not what was picked.** The composer carries its own "Change type", so a player can start a message and finish a dossier. `_adoptEntryType(entry)` runs on collect AND again before the encode: an allowed type is adopted (with the craft skill re-synced, since Forgery is not Accounting), and anything the GM disallows is reverted with a reason rather than rolled at a DV that does not belong to it. Without it a rewritten message was forged at the message's nonexistent DV.

**The chip designer bakes an icon — `scripts/ui/ShardEncoder/ShardIconBuilder.js`.** A framework-free promise modal (the SecurityConsole / OperatorSelect family, mounted into `host` so it wears that window's palette) composing a shape, a colour, and a mark that is either a Font Awesome symbol or any image. The **chip shape is the Encoder core's own silhouette** — the square with the top-left and bottom-right corners cut in, mirroring `.ncm-fw-chip-core`'s `clip-path` on canvas — so the icon on the item is recognisably the thing the player watched compile. Colour is a `CT_COLORS` swatch **or any hex** from a native picker; `hexFor()` takes either, and dragging the picker repaints live without rebuilding the swatch row. The icon is baked onto **`#1b1f21`**, the flat near-black every Foundry item tile sits on — a transparent PNG shows the sheet through the chip and reads as a hole in the row. It resolves a **PNG data URI, not a path**: writing a real file needs upload permission a player usually lacks, while a data URI is a string on an item they already own. **The Font Awesome trick:** a class name carries no character — the glyph lives in the stylesheet's `::before { content }` — so a hidden probe element is mounted, `getComputedStyle(el, '::before').content` is read back, and THAT character is drawn in the FA family. `paintIcon` is shared by the preview, the two small previews and the bake, so what is approved is the pixels that land. A cross-origin image taints the canvas and `toDataURL` throws; that is caught and explained rather than handed back broken.

**A cred drop is money MOVED, not made — `_eddiesLoad()`.** An `eddies` entry needs no roll, so nothing stands between a player and minting eb from nothing; the old `ShardConversionFlow` debited the author's wealth and the Encoder must too. The amount is checked against wealth **before the roll** (together with a bought blank's price, so a player is never told halfway), debited **only once something is really written** (a rejected encode returns before the money moves — the blank is spent, the money is not), re-checked at the moment of writing in case wealth moved, and **refunded with the blank price if the write then fails**. It is stated in three places: the readout sentence, the artifact card, and the encode stage's locked-in list.

**The startup is the player's to write.** The Look row also sets what the shard SAYS when it opens: title, the line under it, one of the nine boot choreographies `ItemInboxApp._bootAnimations` actually implements (offering a tenth would be a dead option — the sim cross-checks the two lists), a speed, and a switch to skip the startup entirely. A preset seeds the animation and the placeholders; typing over them is an override, and `_bootConfig` writes ONLY fields the player filled, so an untouched shard keeps the preset's own words instead of being flattened to the generic defaults.

**Copying shards into the Items directory — Admin → Shards → "Copy Shards to Items".** The recurring question is where a player-made shard shows up for the GM, and the honest answer has two halves: an item **embedded in an actor cannot be foldered at all**, and moving it would take the shard off the player's sheet. So `DataShardService.archiveShardsToItems()` makes **reference copies** as world items, filed under **Player-Made Shards → the character**. Each copy keeps the source's `journalId`, so it is a **live window** on the player's real shard rather than a snapshot that drifts. `surveyShardArchive()` is the read-only census the dialog shows first (what would be copied, what already is, which copies have lost their shard). Re-running is idempotent — a source that already has a copy is skipped, and a copy is never itself copied.
- **The guard that makes this safe:** the admin Unconvert calls `removeDataShard(item, true)`, which deletes the linked journal. An archive shares that journal, so deleting a copy would gut the player's shard — `removeDataShard` now refuses to delete a journal when the item carries `shardArchive`. Never remove that check.

**Player-made shards are filed under their maker.** `MessageRepository.getSubfolderPath([...])` creates a nested journal-folder path, and `convertToDataShard` uses it when `configOverrides.playerMade.actorName` is set: the linked journal lands in **Data Shards / Player-Made / <character>** instead of the flat folder. Folder creation is a world write, so the helper is **GM-only and degrades** — a player gets the deepest folder that already exists and the journal simply stays put, rather than throwing. The **item** cannot be foldered at all (it is embedded in an actor), so the Admin → Shards row carries a **"Made by <character>" badge** instead (gold when the forgery was sloppy), which is also how a GM learns a dossier is a fake before the NPC it accuses does.

**The Agent encodes as the character it is OPEN as.** `MessageViewerApp` has `actorId`, NOT `actor` — the first cut passed `this.actor` (undefined), so the Encoder fell back to `resolveOperator()` and quietly encoded as the remembered character rather than the one whose Agent was on screen. The door now passes `game.actors.get(this.actorId)`, and the Encoder's Operator card shows on EVERY stage (naming the character even where nothing rolls) so the question can never be silent again.

**PERMISSIONS — three of the Encoder's writes are the player's own, and three are not.** Theirs: creating a bought blank on their actor, paying for it out of `system.wealth`, and renaming/restyling the item. Not theirs: the shard journal, the world settings, and the price — all relayed. `_permissionProblem()` checks the player-side ones **up front (on open) and again before the roll**, in one sentence each: no operator · an operator you do not OWN (both the buy and the payment write to that actor) · authoring switched off mid-session. `_createBlank` refuses loudly rather than returning a silent null, the cosmetic item writes are wrapped so a failure cannot cost a shard that was already rolled for, and `EncoderConfigApp.render` refuses for a non-GM at the door instead of failing at Save.

**The LOCK stage is a CEILING, never a check — and it is optional.** A lock roll cannot be failed: the total picks a rung in `cfg.lock.ceilings` (the table the old `ShardConversionFlow` used, now a GM setting), and the rung says the highest encryption DV you may set and which affordances open. A bad roll still locks the chip, it just cannot lock it hard. Two independent, optional rolls: **Cryptography** buys the encryption ceiling and Black ICE (which ALSO needs the GM's `lock.blackIce`), **Electronics/Security Tech** buys the bite-back failure mode (10+) and a key-item reader (20+). A password needs no skill and no roll at all — it is a secret you set.
- **`lock.allowed: false` removes the stage**, it does not grey it out: `_stages()` filters `STAGES`, and the spine, the footer count and the navigation all read that filtered list. Never index `STAGES` directly.
- **`_lockConfig()` writes the shard config's OWN long-standing field names** (`encrypted`/`encryptionType`/`encryptionDC`/`failureMode`, `requiresLogin`/`loginPassword`, `requiresKeyItem`/`keyItemName`…), so a player-locked shard is breached by exactly the same code as a GM-locked one. An untouched lock writes **nothing** — an open shard must not carry half a security block.

**MARKET — the player proposes, the GM disposes (`scripts/ui/ShardEncoder/ShardMarket.js`).** It renders only at the seal, because there is nothing to price until the thing exists. `cfg.pricing`: **off** (never rendered) · **propose** (the default and the dev's locked decision — the figure does NOT touch the item until the GM approves) · **free** · **formula**. The write is always the GM's: `system.price.market` is CPR's field on an item a player may not own, so `propose` relays through **`SHARD_PRICE_REQUEST`** and the GM's own client prompts and writes. **`applyShardPrice` refuses a non-GM caller**, so the mode is enforced where the write happens rather than in the UI. Three failure paths are handled out loud rather than silently: no GM online, a prompt closed without answering (= **deny**, never a silent yes), and no answer inside two minutes.

**`ShardEncoderApp` is hosted in the Malware Workshop FRAME.** Its root classes are `['ncm-app', 'ncm-malware-workshop', 'ncm-shard-encoder']`, so every `.ncm-fw-*` rule IS the real chrome and only `.ncm-enc-*` is new (`styles/components/shard-encoder.css`, imported **after** malware-workshop.css — the sim asserts the order). Build-once-and-patch like the Workshop: the PART is a bare root, `_mount()` builds it and every interaction patches the DOM, never `render()` — which is also what lets the stage transition (out-fold → handoff sweep → core morph → cascade in) animate on live nodes. Two Workshop rules needed a local override: `.ncm-fw-icecard__ic` is **red** there because it means Black ICE (a blank chip is not a threat), and `.ncm-fw-chip` is styled as a SPAN, so a `<button>` wearing it still gets Foundry's white default.

**It is NOT `ADMIN_THEMED`** (it is a player window; the GM config app IS) and the sim asserts both directions.

**Writes:** the player owns the blank item, but a shard journal is GM-owned, so conversion + the first entry ride the existing `SHARD_CONVERT_REQUEST` relay. **`_canPersist()` runs BEFORE the roll** (the sim asserts the ordering) — never after something irreversible. A bought blank debits wealth and **refunds on any failure**. A **bricked** blank is FLAGGED (`flags.<MODULE>.blankBricked` + a renamed item), not deleted — non-destructive, auditable, and `_candidates()` filters bricked chips out so it can never be re-used. The entry is built through **`programToEntry`** (the Workshop's own shape, so the Deck / upload / SWEEP need nothing) and stamped with `provenance.playerMade` + the forge quality; the shard config carries a `playerMade` block for the GM's audit, and `TraceService.logSuccess` writes the Security-tab row when `cfg.audit` is on.

**Three doors, one launcher — `game.nightcity.openEncoder({item?, stage?, family?, actor?})`** (registerReadyServices ready/115, refuses when the GM has not switched authoring on): the Agent's Shards tab **"Encode new"** action pill (`open-encoder`, gated on the context flag `canEncode`), the item right-click (`ShardSheetOverride._launchConversionFlow`, whose door-side gate is `ShardSheetOverride.canAuthor(actor)` → `canEncode`), and the **Program Deck's first empty cartridge** (`.ncm-pd-cart--new`). Every one of them, and the launcher itself, judges through `canEncode` — see the encoder-gate section.

**GM card: Admin → Shards → Player Authoring → `EncoderConfigApp`** (`ADMIN_THEMED`, admin vocabulary: display chrome, mono values, 2px radius, `.ncm-ct-row__check`). Five tabs — Basics (the master switch, pricing, lock, re-encode/copy, audit) · Blanks (tier cards with price / botch rules / perk / an item picker in the **burn-picker pattern**) · What they can make (the 18-type allow-list with craft skill + DV + alt) · Programs (families, gating skill, alts, per-ability DV + rank, charges, trojan cover) · Optional depth. **Nothing is written until Save** (`this.draft` is the working copy), and it builds-once-and-patches because a settings sheet full of live inputs must not lose focus to a re-render.

**ACCESSIBILITY — `scripts/utils/legibility.js` is the ONE place, and any FUI surface can adopt it.** Per-player (client setting `legibilityPrefs`), opened from the Encoder's footer **DISPLAY** button, applied live with no re-render. `applyLegibility(root, prefs)` stamps `--ncm-lg-text` plus `data-lg-motion|density|outlines|font` and writes the contrast palette inline via the shared `applyContrastToElement`. Seven knobs: **text size** (80–160), **contrast** (70–140), **motion** (full/calm/off), **density** (compact/roomy), **stronger box edges**, **plain font**, **always show the numbers** — plus five one-click presets (Default / Large text / High contrast / Calm / Maximum).
- **It sits ON TOP of the theme, it does not replace it.** Text size MULTIPLIES the player's Agent Text Size — the Encoder redefines `--ncm-text-scale: calc(var(--ncm-text-scale-base,1) * var(--ncm-lg-text,1))`, which is the shard-viewer composition trick and means the **borrowed Workshop classes scale too** (they are all `calc(Npx * var(--ncm-text-scale,1))`). Contrast is the same OKLCH tone-map as the Theme Customizer, and **100% removes the inline overrides** so an untouched window keeps following the theme live.
- **Motion can only ever be LOWERED.** `effectiveMotion` forces `off` when the OS asks for reduced motion or the module's animation pref is off; the player's choice can never raise it. Every beat of the encode sequence reads it (`off` = instant, `calm` = shorter with no looping decoration).
- **Plain font works by redefining the font slots** (`--ncm-font-display/title/mono/ui` → the body face) plus one `text-transform:none` sweep, so it reaches the borrowed chrome without touching a single component rule.

**First live run (2026-09-21) — four fixes worth keeping in mind.** (1) The blank list has a **search + filter chips**: the search filters the LIVE DOM (re-rendering the rail per keystroke steals focus), and the chips are **one per item TYPE the character actually carries** (Gear, Cyberware, Weapons…, labelled by `_typeLabel`) plus All and Blanks. They render whenever the character carries anything — the first cut only showed them when the GM had registered blank items, so a fresh world had no filter at all. (2) **The player composer must not demand an item** — a BOUGHT blank has none until the encode succeeds, and requiring one showed the GM-only "No shard selected" panel, so player mode renders and saves without one. (3) **Every type tile names its skill**, not just the selected one — otherwise you have to click all eleven to learn which trade each wants. (4) **A finished shard has to be findable**: the item directory, the actor sheet and the Agent SHARDS tab all cache their lists, so `_refreshShardViews()` repaints them, the seal's button becomes **Open it**, and the hint names whose gear it is in.

**Test setup:** `tools/test-encoder-setup.macro.js` — paste into a Script macro as GM: switches player authoring on, puts one blank of each TIER on a carrier actor and registers them by name in their tiers, then prints a what-to-try list (the three doors, a forgery, a rejected/bricked blank, the program branch, the accessibility panel, the two-client relay test).

**Verification:** `node tools/sims/test-encoder-model.mjs` (165 checks — config layering, tiers, the ladder, program gating/efficiency, the content branch (no-roll types, the scan-DV formula, the collect-not-persist contract), the lock ceilings (monotonic, never empty, the GM veto), the market (GM-only writes, deny-on-close, no-GM), the legibility clamps, and the wiring a window can get wrong: stylesheet order, template registration, class-prefix collisions, admin-theme flags, relay-before-roll). Phase 6 gained **6.14** (model + settings + ladder agreement + every offered verb has an applier) and **6.15** (legibility clamps and stamps). Visual: **`docs/mockups/shard-encoder-probe.html`** mounts the REAL app against the real cascade (`?stage=blank|content|program|lock|encode`, `?type=`, `?wrote=1`, `?rolled=`, `?sealed=clean|sloppy`, `?preset=large|contrast|calm|max`, `?display=1`, `?still=1`, `?h=`) and reports contrast / clipping / the stamped prefs in `<title>`. **`?still=1` matters**: headless advances JS timers but NOT CSS animations, so an entrance cascade captures frozen at opacity 0.

## Architecture: shards players don't own — the state relay, the editing rule, re-encode, Permissions (2026-10-21)

Tester report: "to modify a shard a player needs Owner rights, and Owner puts the shard in the Items sidebar before its reveal" + "we haven't done anything to update existing shards". Both were real; they are two different problems.

- **A shard that arrives as a message attachment is a WORLD item the player has no permission on, and every state write a player's hack caused went straight to `item.update`** — `_handleHackSuccess` (decrypted), `_updateActorSession` (attempts / lockouts / login / key item), `_decryptMessage` (a page on the GM-owned journal), `_destroyShard`. The roll succeeded, the write threw, the shard looked like it never unlocked — hence "needs Owner". **Now every one of them goes through `_canWriteItem(item)` (GM or `item.isOwner`) → direct, else `_relayState(op, item, payload)` → `SOCKET_OPS.SHARD_STATE_REQUEST`** (ops `decrypt` | `session {actorId, updates}` | `decryptPage {messageId}` | `destroy`; GM handler `handleStateRequest`, one GM lands it; no GM online → warns + returns false). A shard on the player's own sheet is still a direct write (they own the actor). **So a world shard needs NO ownership at all** — leave it at None, it stays out of the sidebar, and the player hacks it through the Agent. Don't reintroduce a bare `item.update` on a player-reachable path; the sim greps for it.
- **Per-shard editing rule — `config.playerEditing`: `locked` | `maker` | `qualified`** (Configure Shard → Security → "Player Editing"; `SHARD_EDIT_MODES` + the pure `resolveShardEditing(config)` in EncoderModel.js). **A missing value resolves by provenance: player-made (`playerMade.actorId`) → maker only, everything else → locked** — so every GM-made shard, and every pre-existing one, is read-only for players with no migration and nothing to remember. It governs WRITING only (add a file / re-encode); hacking, claiming, cracking and reading are play, not modification, and never consult it. **`canEditShard(item, actor)` (helpers.js) is the ONE door-side answer**: GM → ok; the shard's rule; then `canEncode`; then `encoderConfig.reencode.allowed`. Doors: the shard viewer's + button (`canPlayerEdit` → a player's `addEntry` opens the Encoder, never the GM composer), the item right-click "Encode More Onto It" (directory + inventory row), `_launchConversionFlow` on an already-converted item, and the Encoder itself (`_permissionProblem` re-judges before the roll). Admin → Shards rows wear an **Editable · maker/qualified** badge when the rule is open.
- **Re-encode is REAL now** — `encoderConfig.reencode` was a dead knob (the GM card ticked "Rewrite a sloppy shard" and nothing read it). The Encoder has a third source, **`srcMode: 'edit'`** ("Re-encode a shard": carried shards passing `canEditShard`; a shard handed to `openEncoder({item})` opens there). Rules: the LOCK stage is absent (`_stages()` drops it — the lock was rolled when the shard was made), the Look row is hidden, DV = stack DV + `reencode.dv`, room = the chip's own stamped `config.capacity` minus what is on it (`_onChip()`), a rejection **never bricks** (`_botch()` → "the shard is unchanged"; every botch read goes through it), and the write is **`DataShardService.reencodeShard(item, {…, entries})`** (GM direct; player rides `SHARD_CONVERT_REQUEST` with a `reencode` block — the handler branches BEFORE converting): the new quality + `sniffDV` are re-stamped on every **player-made** entry only (a GM-authored entry on a shard the GM opened up is never touched), `config.playerMade` keeps the original maker/date and gains `reencodedAt/reencodes`, new files go through `addMessage` so capacity is enforced and a refusal is reported.
- **Permissions button** (Admin → Shards row + expanded actions, world items only — an embedded item has no ownership of its own): `shardOwnership` → `new DocumentOwnershipConfig(item)` (bare lexical reference with a typeof guard + the v13 namespace fallback, the ImagePopout lesson). It is for the GM who wants to hand a shard over outright; a player hacking one does not need it any more.
- Also: the Agent's SHARDS tab reads a received world shard's lock state LIVE (`config.encrypted && !state.decrypted`) instead of the attachment's as-sent stamp. Sim: `tools/sims/test-shard-edit-relay.mjs` (38 — the writers as GM / owning player / non-owner, the handler, the rule matrix, `reencodeShard`, the Encoder door, every surface). Phase 6 gained **6.17**. **Not live-tested.** Known limit: the composer's Attach Shard picker is still a raw inline-styled list (not the shared name picker).

## Architecture: the program-effect registry (what a program can DO)

**`PROGRAM_EFFECTS` in `ContentRenderers.js` is the ONE source of truth for every effect a payload executes, a malware deploys, or a resident fires.** Accessors: **`programEffectInfo(key)`** (unknown/legacy → `adjudicate`) and **`programEffectOptions({mode, host, exclude})`** for pickers.

Before it (fixed 2026-07-26) the catalog was duplicated across **five** places that disagreed — the payload metadata map, a *second* hardcoded option list in `DataShardComposer._plContext`, the upload verb list, `_EXEC_DESC` in `TerminalViewerApp`, and the two appliers — and **7 of the 11 verbs had no label anywhere** (`wipe`/`corrupt`/`forgeAccount`/`plant`/`keylog`/`deployICE`/`logicBomb`), which is exactly why they never reached a picker and why **malware could not be authored with an ability at all**. `PAYLOAD_EFFECTS`, `payloadEffectInfo` and `_EXEC_DESC` are gone; don't reintroduce a local list.

Per entry: `label`/`icon`/`blurb` · **`modes`** — `'instant'` (applied by `SiphonFlow._applyNativeEffect`) and/or `'triggered'` (applied by `TerminalService._fireResidentEffect`) · `host` (`'terminal'` when it only means something on a machine) · `group` (picker grouping) · **`params`** (the fields the applier reads out of `effect.params`, so an authoring UI can render them — previously `effect.params` had NO authoring path anywhere).

- **The two appliers are the authority; the registry mirrors them.** A verb must never claim a `mode` whose applier has no `case` for it. The payload manifest's **`isNative` keys on `modes.includes('instant')`, NOT mere presence in the catalog** — with the catalog now holding triggered-only verbs, a presence check would let a payload claim native support it falls through to `adjudicate` on.
- **A verb whose params can't be authored yet stays OUT of its picker.** `logicBomb` is excluded from the upload verb list (`exclude: ['plant','adjudicate','logicBomb']`) because with no `inner` it installs and then no-ops (`'logic bomb had no payload'`) — a dead option is worse than no option.
- Sim: scratchpad `test-registry.mjs` (45/45) asserts **both** directions against the appliers' real case lists, including the negatives.

**Resident triggers ride the trace funnel.** `onLogin`/`onDate`/`onAction` were implemented with live reapers but only `onLogin` could ever be *produced*, and `onAction` had exactly ONE call site (exfil). Now **`TraceService._fireResidentTriggers`** (called from `recordTrace`) reaches every gated action, since they all already report there with an `action` tag + host — no per-site calls. Added **`onHeat`** (`TerminalService.checkHeatResidents`, threshold `trigger.atLeast`) off the heat that funnel already tracks. Rules: **`onAction` fires on SUCCESS only** (a failed attempt is the trace's business, not the bomb's); **`'exfil'` is deliberately EXCLUDED from the funnel path** because its own `EVENTS.TERMINAL_EXFIL` listener owns it *and* catches ungated exfils that never reach the funnel — running both would propose the same resident twice. `SecurityTab`'s resident rows only label `onDate`/`onLogin`; the two new kinds fall through to the raw kind string (cosmetic, unfixed).

## Architecture: embedded media (audio/video)

`resolveMediaEmbed(rawUrl, mediaType)` in `helpers.js` is the **single** source resolver, shared by shards and normal messages. It returns `{ kind: 'none'|'native'|'embed', src, isVideo }`: YouTube/Vimeo links → `embed` (iframe, `src` rebuilt from the parsed video ID — never raw user HTML); direct file/URL → `native` (`<video>`/`<audio>`, audio-vs-video inferred from extension, else `mediaType`); non-`http(s)` schemes (`javascript:`/`data:`) → `none`. Two surfaces:
- **Shard AV Log** content type — `mediaUrl` field added to the composer (`DataShardComposer`, with a `browseMedia` FilePicker) and the player wizard. `ItemInboxApp._enrichEntry` resolves it; `item-inbox.hbs` renders player-or-waveform.
- **Normal messages** — a new media **attachment** (`{ isMedia: true, mediaUrl, mediaType }`) via the composer's "Embed Audio/Video" toolbar action (`_onAttachMedia`). Reuses the existing `attachments[]` persistence (no repo/service change). `MessageViewerApp` splits `isMedia` attachments out of the cards/encrypted-classifier and `_buildMediaEmbeds` renders inline players in `message-detail.hbs` (normal/unlocked branch only — locked/encrypted messages don't leak the player).

## Architecture: sound feedback

All audio goes through `SoundService.play(id, {volume,loop})` (IDs in `SOUND_PATHS`), which gates on the `soundEnabled` pref and scales by `soundVolume` — that one NCM slider governs **everything** below.

**UI interaction sounds are centralized in `BaseApplication`** (every NCM window extends it) — do **not** sprinkle `play('click')` in handlers (that drifted inconsistent; the manual calls were removed). Wired once via `_wireUiSounds()`: a document-level **capture** listener with a `this.element.contains()` containment guard (element-level listeners are unreliable in Foundry), torn down in `close()`. So only the window containing the event plays, and capture means a handler's `stopPropagation` can't swallow it. Coverage: `click` on actionable controls (`button, [data-action], a[href], [role="button"], .ncm-clickable`), `open` (first render), `close`. `EmailSetupFlow` extends raw `ApplicationV2` and is **not** covered.

**There is no hover SFX** — it was deliberately removed (the `mouseover` listener, the `#uiHoverHandler`/`#lastHover` fields, and the `'hover'` entry in `SOUND_PATHS` are all gone; `assets/sounds/ui/hover.ogg` lingers unused). Don't re-add a hover sound. CSS `:hover` styles and hover-*preview* UI logic (trust segments, date-range picker) are unrelated and stay.

**The click is varied + quiet** via `SoundService.playClick()`: base `CLICK_GAIN = 0.25`, plus per-press random pitch (playback rate 0.94–1.06) and volume jitter (0.85–1.15×) using a one-shot Web Audio buffer source (the click `.ogg` decoded once + cached), so repeats don't sound identical. Falls back to `play('click', {volume})` if Web Audio is unavailable. `soundVolume` is baked into the gain so the slider still applies. Tuning knobs: `CLICK_GAIN` + the jitter ranges in `playClick()`. The same Web Audio path has a public face — **`SoundService.playStyled(id, {volume, rate})`** — used by the per-terminal sound sets (see the terminals section) to re-pitch existing assets; rate 1 short-circuits to the standard player.

**Shard/security outcome audio: the animated UI owns the climax, not the service.** `DataShardService.attemptHack` and `validateKeyItem` accept a `silent` option; the `ItemInboxApp` calls that drive an animation pass `silent: true` so the service skips its immediate result sounds (which otherwise fire ~6s early / double the animation). The breach sequence (`_playHackSequence`) plays `hack-start` → `hack-progress` (scan + inject) → at the climax `hack-success` **or** `hack-fail` + `black-ice` (on damage) + `lockout` (on lockout); the key-item accept splash plays `key-accepted`, the reject shake plays `key-rejected`. **Non-animated / macro-API callers leave `silent` off and keep the immediate sounds** (`silent` also threads into `_applyBlackICEDamage` → `ICEService.applyDamage`). The **"Shard Unlocked" splash plays `shard-decrypt`** — deliberately distinct from `hack-success` (which is the dice-roll climax, not the shard opening). The 5 once-orphaned sounds are now wired: `hack-start`/`hack-progress` (sequence), `shard-compile` (encoding overlay), `shard-expire` (`_destroyShard`), `trace-fire` (`NotificationService.showShardTrace`).

## Architecture: templates

All `.hbs` paths are constants in `TEMPLATES` (in `scripts/utils/constants.js`) and preloaded by `scripts/init/registerTemplates.js`. **A "Partial not found" error at render time means the file isn't in `TEMPLATES`** — add it there.

CSS is a single entry: `styles/night-city-messenger.css` `@import`s every other stylesheet. New stylesheets must be added there.

## Foundry v12 / ApplicationV2 gotchas

These have all bitten the project. Don't relearn them:

- `data-action` on `<select>` fires on **click** (dropdown opens), not on value change. Add a manual `addEventListener('change', ...)` for selects.
- Element-level `addEventListener` is unreliable in Foundry (DOM focus issues). Use `document.addEventListener` with an app containment guard, and remove it in a `close()` override.
- `item.update()` uses `mergeObject` on nested flag paths — for reliable resets, do `await item.unsetFlag(...)` then `await item.setFlag(...)`.
- `Dialog` callbacks don't return values to a Promise — use a mutable variable in the callback, read it after `close: () => resolve()`.
- `FormDataExtended` is unreliable with selects/checkboxes in ApplicationV2 — read DOM directly via `querySelector`.
- ApplicationV2 template parts must have a single root element.
- `render(true)` forces a template re-render; `render(false)` only repositions.
- `CONTACT_DECRYPTED` and similar EventBus events fire **synchronously** inside their producer methods. Use a guard flag (e.g. `_breachInProgress`) to prevent premature renders that detach animated DOM.
- `_onRender()` is the only safe post-render DOM manipulation point. Use a `_pendingReveal` flag pattern when you need to defer animation work to the next render.
- **Static methods aren't on the prototype.** `this.foo()` from inside another static method (where `this` is the bound instance) silently fails when `foo` is also `static` — `this.foo` returns `undefined`. If a method needs to be invoked via `this.x()` from a static context (such as ApplicationV2's static action handlers), it must be an instance method. This bit `ContactsTab._showShareDialog` and was a latent bug for an unknown amount of time.
- **A button's `disabled`/enabled state driven by a template variable won't update for live inputs that don't re-render.** Sliders, color pickers, `<select>`s, and text fields wired via `addEventListener` typically mutate working state without a `render()`, so a `{{#unless dirty}}disabled{{/unless}}` button stays stuck. Toggle the `disabled` attribute (and the disabled class) directly in the DOM from the change handler. This bit the Theme Customizer Save button — fixed via `_markDirty()`, which sets the flag *and* pokes the button.
- **`../` in a template counts BLOCKS, not object levels — and `{{#if}}` is a block.** Handlebars pushes a depth frame for every block it enters, so `{{#each ../list}}` inside two nested `{{#if}}`s (an `{{#if}}/{{else if}}` chain counts too) walks out past the root and resolves to **nothing** — no error, just an empty loop. That is what rendered the GM Contact Manager's detail-panel Share select as a "Players" heading with no one under it, while the identical top-bar select (same data, one frame shallower, no `../`) worked. **Reach for root data with `@root.x`**, which is depth-proof; keep `../` for stepping out of an `{{#each}}` you are actually inside. A bare name also works — it searches the current frame — but only until someone nests the block one deeper. Sim: `tools/sims/test-gm-share-targets.mjs` compiles the REAL template through Handlebars and asserts each select lists the characters (it judges each select separately, since a whole-document search let the working one cover for the broken one).
- **A Font Awesome 6 glyph class alone renders NOTHING — it needs the FAMILY class too.** `<i class="fa-gun">` has a `::before` content rule but no font-family; only `fas`/`fa-solid` supplies it. `getRoleIcon` (designHelpers.js) returned bare `fa-x` for years, so every role chip on player contact cards / detail / list rows was an empty gap (tester 2026-10-21: "solo and netrunner icons don't appear"). It now returns the WHOLE class (`fas fa-crosshairs`), its built-in table mirrors the GM's role table (RoleManagerDialog `BUILT_IN_ROLES`) so a role looks the same on both sides, and GM custom roles resolve through `masterContactService.getCustomRoles()`. The Contact Manager's private `roleIcons` copy is gone. Sim: `tools/sims/test-role-icons.mjs`. Rule: an icon string that reaches a template is a full class, never a bare glyph name.
- **A context FIELD named like a registered Handlebars HELPER never prints — `{{sub}}` CALLS the `sub` helper (subtraction) with no arguments and renders `NaN`** (the group tray's member line, tester 2026-10-21). Handlebars resolves a bare mustache as a helper first. registerTemplates.js registers `eq neq gt lt gte lte and or not add sub letter join lower upper truncate substring…`, so none of those is a safe field name. `tools/sims/test-helper-collisions.mjs` reads the helper list and fails on any bare `{{name}}` / `{{#if name}}` use of one across every template.
- **Script macros run with `speaker, actor, token, character, scope` predefined** as the macro function's parameters. Declaring a local `const actor`/`token`/`character` is a redeclaration error — use other names (`_who`, etc.). Foundry wraps the body in an **async** function, so top-level `await`/`return` are legal (validate macro source the same way — see Compendium packs).

## CSS specificity patterns

- Foundry's globals require `!important` on most `ncm-actlog__` and Network Manager rules.
- `border:` shorthand with `!important` kills inline `border-color` — split into `border-width` + `border-style`.
- `background:` shorthand with `!important` kills `background-image` SVG arrows on selects — split into `background-color` + `background-image`.
- CSS custom properties must be scoped to `:root` (not `.ncm-app`) for ThemeService overrides to apply.
- Foundry button reset must be element+class at specificity 0,2,1 — kill structural defaults only (height, min-height, appearance, box-shadow, margin, background-image), never visual properties.
- `overflow: hidden` vs `visible` is a tradeoff — `visible` fixes dropdowns but breaks flex scroll chains. Resolve by ensuring dropdowns open into a non-clipped area.
- **Foundry's global `.flexrow > *, .flexcol > * { flex: 1 }`** stretches every direct child of a flex row. Anything you inject into a Foundry sheet/sidebar (badges, wrap spans, custom widgets) needs `flex: 0 0 auto !important` or it will fill the row and push sibling content aside. The `.ncm-shard-thumb-wrap` span around inventory data-shard thumbnails is the canonical example.
- **`display: inline-block` on a flex item can render invisible.** When the wrap also has `line-height: 0` (or other line-box-collapsing styles), the element computes to zero size. Use `display: inline-flex` (with `align-items: center`) instead — it gives the element its own flex context and sizes reliably to its content.
- **CSS variable typos render as invisible, not error.** `var(--missing-name)` with no fallback resolves to the property's initial value (often `transparent` for backgrounds/borders, blank for colors). If a UI element looks empty/blank, grep `styles/base/variables.css` to confirm the var name. The actual NCM names are `--ncm-primary`, `--ncm-secondary`, `--ncm-accent` — there is **no** `--ncm-color-*` prefix. The blank shard preset's loading bar was invisible for this reason.
- **Standalone (non-admin) NCM windows still need explicit Foundry button/input resets.** A bare button (e.g. `.ncm-aip__row`) renders with Foundry's white default; scope rules as `.ncm-app button.<class> { … !important }` and reset `appearance` / `box-shadow` / `background` / `height`. This bit the ActorImportPickerApp.
- **A bare `<hr>` renders with the Cyberpunk RED system's gradient, not your border.** The CPR system styles `hr` with a gradient `background`/`border-image`; a divider rule that only sets `border-top` lets that gradient show through underneath. To force a flat line, raise specificity (`.ncm-app hr.<class>`) and add `background: none !important; border-image: none !important; height: 0`. This bit `.ncm-form-divider` (the "GM-ONLY FIELDS" divider in the contacts form).
- **Decorative gradient accents were deliberately flattened to solid lines** (per dev preference). Converted: the player Contacts app top accent (`.ncm-contacts-v5::before`), the date-range picker top accent (`.ncm-time-config-dialog::after` in `tab-tools.css`), and the GM Contact Manager top bar (`.ncm-gm-bar`) + top accent line (`.ncm-gm-contacts::before`). All use solid `var(--ncm-*)` now. If adding new chrome accents, prefer a flat line over a `linear-gradient`.
- **Foundry defaults `<button>` to `width: 100%`.** Converting an inline `<span>` badge/pill into a `<button>` (to make it clickable) makes it stretch full-width — and a flex-row parent stretches it further. Add `width: auto !important; flex: 0 0 auto !important`. This bit `.ncm-viewer__detail-thread` (the Thread pill) the moment it became a button.
- **Search fields: use the flex pattern, not an absolutely-positioned icon.** An `position:absolute` magnifier over a padded input overlaps the placeholder in Foundry. Instead use a bordered flex wrapper holding the `<i>` as a sibling beside a borderless, `padding:0` input — see `.ncm-net-config-search` / `.ncm-scene-ctl__search-wrap`.
- **Tooltips use Foundry's native `data-tooltip` (+ optional `data-tooltip-direction="UP|DOWN|LEFT|RIGHT"`), NOT CSS-pseudo tooltips.** Foundry renders `#tooltip` on `document.body`, so it escapes the window and auto-flips at edges. The old `data-ncm-tooltip` CSS-pseudo approach was clipped by `.window-content { overflow:hidden }` (pseudo-elements can't escape an overflow-hidden ancestor; the contrast `filter` + viewer `container-type` made fixed-escape impossible too — note the contrast `filter` has since been removed in favor of OKLCH palette tone-mapping, but `container-type` remains, so the native-tooltip decision stands). All usages were migrated; the dead `[data-ncm-tooltip]` rules linger inert in `styles/base/interactions.css`.
- **Text elements must declare `font-family: var(--ncm-font-*)` to follow themes.** A rule with no `font-family` silently inherits Foundry's default and won't respond to the Theme Customizer's font selection — it looks fine until a player switches fonts and that one element stays put. The slots are `--ncm-font-title`/`--ncm-font-ui` (Orbitron chrome), `--ncm-font-display`/`--ncm-font-body` (Rajdhani; use for prominent names/subjects), `--ncm-font-mono` (Share Tech Mono; technical values only). This bit `.ncm-viewer__detail-sender-name`, which had only `font-size`/`color` and ignored theme fonts.
- **`overflow-y: auto` with no explicit `overflow-x` spawns a phantom horizontal scrollbar.** Per the CSS overflow spec, setting one axis to a non-`visible` value computes the other (`visible`) axis as `auto` — so a 1px content overrun shows a horizontal scrollbar. Always pair them: add `overflow-x: hidden` next to any `overflow-y: auto`. This bit the date-range picker dialog content (`.ncm-time-config-dialog .window-content` in `tab-tools.css`).
- **Indentation comes from a section wrapper, not the row.** In the Theme Customizer, rows get their 16px horizontal inset from a padded `.ncm-tc__font-section` (`padding: 8px 16px`) / `.ncm-tc__slabel`/`.ncm-tc__name-hint` (`… 16px`), NOT from `.ncm-tc__srow` itself (which is `padding: 6px 0`). A `.ncm-tc__srow` placed directly in the panel (outside a section) sits flush-left and misaligns — wrap it in a section. This bit the Agent Name row.
- **Text is unselectable by default — Foundry core AND the CPR system both ship `* { user-select: none }` resets** (foundry2.css `@layer reset` and the CPR style.css Theme-V1 rules). Every player-readable surface must OPT IN, and because a directly-matched `* { none }` beats an *inherited* `text`, the opt-in must hit descendants directly: `.container, .container * { user-select: text }`, then re-disable on `button`/`[data-action]` so clicks don't smear selections. Blocks live in message-viewer.css (`.ncm-viewer__detail-body *`, the original), item-inbox.css (`.ncm-item-inbox *`), terminal.css (`.ncm-terminal-viewer *`, keypad excluded), and content-types.css (`.ncm-entry-body *` / `[class*="ncm-ct-"] *` — covers typed content everywhere incl. the composer preview). New windows that display copyable text need the same block ("all the text isn't selectable" was the 2026-07-11 bug report).
- **The `hidden` attribute loses to any author `display` rule.** `[hidden]`'s UA style is plain `display:none`, so a class like `.ncm-cfg-row { display:flex }` makes a `hidden` row render anyway. Pair toggle-by-`hidden` markup with `.<scope> [hidden] { display:none !important }` (see `.ncm-acct-editor` in terminal.css — the custom-clearance and datetime rows rendered visible without it).
- **A relative `url()` inside a CSS custom property resolves against the stylesheet that USES the `var()`, not the document base.** Setting `--x: url('worlds/img.webp')` via `setProperty` and consuming it in `styles/components/*.css` fetches `/modules/…/styles/components/worlds/img.webp` → silent 404 → "the image just doesn't show". Root the path first (`foundry.utils.getRoute`, typeof-guarded). This was the terminal-wallpaper bug.
- **A flat `position: relative` on a selector list can silently override a member's existing `position: absolute`** (equal specificity — later in the file wins), collapsing an `inset:0` overlay into normal flow ("the card jumped to the top"). Wrap positioning *defaults* in `:where(…)` (zero specificity) so they can never out-order a real scheme. Related: to slot a decorative pseudo UNDER an element's content without touching the children, give the host `isolation: isolate` and the pseudo `z-index: -1` — never blanket `> * { position:relative; z-index:1 }`, which also flattens any child's deliberate higher z-index (the login hero's dropdown).
- **AppV2 root classes are SAME-ELEMENT — `.ncm-app .ncm-<window>` descendant selectors are DEAD when `<window>` is an app root class.** `DEFAULT_OPTIONS.classes: ['ncm-app', 'ncm-data-shard-composer']` both land on the ONE application root, so the descendant form never matches; write `.ncm-app.ncm-data-shard-composer` (compound, no space). It works for OTHER windows only because their inner template root repeats the class (`.ncm-item-inbox`, `.ncm-admin-panel`, `.ncm-viewer` are template-root classes) — the composer's template root is `.ncm-composer-shell`, so its ENTIRE `.ncm-app .ncm-data-shard-composer` normalization block (checkbox 13px, input 22px, DV max-width, text-align:left — the #25b/#31 fixes) silently never shipped until 2026-07-13. Symptom: "fixed" sizing bugs the dev keeps reporting; headless repros pass because they hand-build nested divs.
- **A themed `<select>` in a body-mounted admin Dialog still has WHITE options unless you style `option` too.** Foundry core sets `select option { background: var(--color-select-option-bg) }` and that var is **`transparent`**, so Chromium paints the popup white while the options inherit the select's text colour → on the dark admin presets, white-on-white until hover (the Shards tab's Quick Create / Convert Item pickers, tester 2026-10-20; "fine on the light theme" because text-primary is dark there). AppV2 windows are covered by `.ncm-app select option` (contact-manager.css); dialogs are not — admin-theme.css now carries `.ncm-admin-dialog[data-ncm-admin-theme] .window-content select option`. Any NEW select host outside `.ncm-app` needs its own `option` rule.
- **A fixed-px `height` on a control whose `font-size` is `calc(Npx * --ncm-text-scale)` CLIPS when the player raises Text Size.** The font sweeps scaled the glyphs but left `height: 26px !important` boxes behind — Encoder inputs, the player entry composer's `.ncm-config-input`/number boxes, the schematic pin popover, the composer's net-search + encryption-DV inputs, the viewer's category-pill strip (tester: "text box is not sizing and cuts off text"). Rule: **size a scaled control's height with the same `calc()`** (`height: calc(26px * var(--ncm-text-scale, 1))`). Admin-tagged windows force the scale to 1, so GM sheets were never affected — test with the Theme Customizer slider or the Encoder's DISPLAY panel.
- **Browser password managers offer the player's FORGE login on an in-fiction login screen (tester, 2026-10-20).** A text field followed by a `type="password"` field is the shape every manager pattern-matches as a sign-in form for the current ORIGIN (forge-vtt.com), and `autocomplete="off"` is deliberately ignored for logins. Every secret field in the module (shard login, terminal manual/lock/keypad/net-join, the network auth dialog, the composer's credentials card) now carries **`autocomplete="new-password"`** (the one value Chrome/Edge/Firefox honour — no saved-credential fill), the extension opt-outs (`data-lpignore` / `data-1p-ignore` / `data-bwignore` / `data-form-type="other"`), and a field `name` that no longer says username/password/login (`ncm-shard-op`/`ncm-shard-key`, `ncm-term-op`/`ncm-term-key`/`ncm-term-netkey`, `ncm-net-key`) — the name is a heuristic on its own. **Second layer, because the attributes are only a hint:** `guardSecretInputs(root)` (helpers.js, called from the shard viewer's, terminal viewer's and network-auth dialog's `_onRender`) makes every secret field and handle **readonly until the first pointerdown/keydown** — no browser autofills a readonly field, and the guard lifts before the key's default action so the keystroke lands (focus alone does not lift it: an autofocused field would lift at render). `input.ncm-autofill-guard[readonly]` in interactions.css keeps it looking live. **Third layer, the one managers cannot see (dev, 2026-10-21: Chrome's manager AND NordPass still offered the Forge login after the attributes, the readonly guard AND a CSS-masked text field — Chromium's form parser treats a text field carrying `-webkit-text-security` as a password field, and NordPass keys on a "Username" label):** `shadowSecretInput(el)` (helpers.js, applied by `guardSecretInputs`) makes the field a **plain text input whose DISPLAYED value is bullets painted by the module**; the real text lives in script and is served through an instance override of the element's `value` property, so every reader (`input.value`) and writer (the keypad's `input.value = …`) works unchanged, and `beforeinput` keeps typing / backspace / paste in sync. Nothing on the element says password: no type, no CSS mask, no label (the shard login reads **Operator / Passcode** and the terminal prompt **OPERATOR** — after "Account" + a "handle" name + an "admin" placeholder still drew NordPass to the name field, nothing on that field may read as a username to a manager: label, `name`, placeholder (the name fields carry `data-ncm-login`, which is what the guard selects). **And then NordPass still attached to the plain text field whatever it was called**, so `shadowLoginInput(el)` replaces the name `<input>` with a **contenteditable element** dressed in the input's computed style, mirroring into the original input turned `type="hidden"` — readers that query `[name="ncm-shard-op"]` are untouched, Enter moves focus to the passcode, paste is flattened to one line. Managers attach to inputs; they do not attach to editable text. **The passcode got the same treatment next** (NordPass moved to the shadowed text input the moment the name field stopped being one): `shadowSecretInput` now also builds a contenteditable (`.ncm-secret-ce`) in front of the input turned `type="hidden"`, shows bullets, serves the real text through the hidden input's `value`, and **forwards keydown Enter to the hidden input** so the terminal / net-join / auth-dialog Enter listeners still fire. Net: a player login screen contains NO visible `<input>` at all). The auth dialog's eye calls `el.ncmSetMasked(false)`. **Never add `-webkit-text-security` or `type="password"` back to a player login field.** A new secret field = same three things + a `guardSecretInputs` call in its window's `_onRender`; `tools/sims/test-login-autofill.mjs` fails on any `type="password"` without them, or any input on a player login screen whose name matches username|password|login (GM config sheets, where the GM types the password a shard will demand, are text fields on an admin window and are not in scope).
- **Foundry v12 checkboxes are `appearance:none` + FontAwesome `::before/::after` glyphs sized by `--checkbox-size` (default 20px).** Forcing only `width`/`height` leaves a 20px glyph overflowing the smaller box = "uncentered checkboxes". Set the VAR (`--checkbox-size: 13px`) — the glyph's width/height/font-size/line-height all read it. `accent-color` does nothing on these; the check color is `--checkbox-checked-color` (core defaults it amber — set it to `--ncm-secondary` for brand).
- **Foundry core `button > i { margin-right: .25rem }` shoves the glyph off-center on icon-ONLY buttons** (the AV ▶, remove ✕s, browse/dice chips). Fix with `button i:only-child { margin: 0 !important; line-height: 1 !important; }` scoped per surface (composer root + `.ncm-entry-body`/`[class*="ncm-ct-"]` for typed content) — icon+text buttons keep the core gap. Headless repros MUST include the real `foundry2.css` (+ FA css + CPR `main.css`, all file:///-loadable) or none of these core interactions reproduce.
- **Scrollbars are themed CENTRALLY in `styles/base/scrollbars.css` — never write scrollbar CSS in a component again.** Two Foundry traps made this necessary: (1) core ships `* { scrollbar-width: thin; scrollbar-color: … }`, and although `scrollbar-color` is an *inherited* property, a **directly-matched** declaration beats an inherited one — so core's `*` re-asserted its bar on every descendant and NCM's old `.ncm-app { scrollbar-color }` never reached anything inside the window (the exact `user-select: none` trap); (2) in Chromium, **specifying `scrollbar-color` DISABLES all `::-webkit-scrollbar-*` styling** for that element — so every webkit scrollbar rule in the module (26 files' worth) was dead code and had been for as long as they've existed. The fix matches descendants directly (`.ncm-app *`, plus the non-`.ncm-app` dialog roots and the body-mounted modals) and colors from `--ncm-*`; because custom properties resolve from the nearest ancestor, a **terminal automatically gets its preset's scrollbar** and the Admin Panel gets the admin theme's, with zero per-surface rules. Per-surface override: set `--ncm-scrollbar-thumb` / `--ncm-scrollbar-track` on the scroller or an ancestor. To hide one, `scrollbar-width: none` at specificity ≥ 0,2,0. Verified against the real core+CPR cascade (brand/terminal presets/admin/override all distinct, non-NCM panes untouched).
- **Never force `overflow: visible` on a Dialog's `.window-content` — it kills the only scroller a v1 Dialog has.** Foundry's `.app { max-height: 100% }` caps the frame at the viewport and `.window-app .window-content { overflow: hidden auto }` is what lets a tall body scroll; `overflow: visible` ALSO flips the content's flex `min-height` from 0 to its full height, so nothing shrinks and the frame's `overflow: hidden` silently eats the footer. Invisible at 100% Text Size (the body fits); at 120% the player-dialog zoom makes the breach dialog taller than the screen and the Execute/Abort buttons vanish with no scrollbar (tester 2026-10-22; Tab still reached them because focus scrolls a hidden-overflow box, and then nothing could scroll back). `.ncm-hack-dialog` and `.ncm-token-picker-dialog` now clip the frame + content, make `.dialog-content` the one scroller, and pin `.dialog-buttons` (`flex: 0 0 auto`). Probe: a static harness in the session scratchpad measured the real cascade at zoom 1/1.2/1.5. A `::-webkit-scrollbar { width: 0 }` on a dialog is dead under scrollbars.css anyway — don't add one.
- **In a modal that scrolls, make the CONTENT LIST the scroller — never the panel.** If the panel itself scrolls, its pinned furniture (a progress/verify strip, the footer buttons) scrolls out of view and the user picking an item near the top sees "nothing happen". Panel = `display:flex; flex-direction:column; overflow:hidden`; the list = `flex:1 1 auto; min-height:0; overflow-y:auto; overflow-x:hidden`; and the list's **children need `flex: 0 0 auto`** or they shrink and their contents overlap (the same shrink gotcha as the composer sections). This bit the OperatorSelect roster.
- **A CLASS-NAME COLLISION between two module stylesheets is the most expensive CSS bug here, because it presents as "a mystery framework rule".** `styles/` is one flat namespace: two files both scoped under `.ncm-app`/`.ncm-admin-panel` land at the SAME specificity (0,2,0), so the later `@import` wins **per declared property** and the newcomer **silently inherits every property it does not re-declare**. The Security tab shipped as `.ncm-sec-card`, colliding with security-overlays.css's `.ncm-app .ncm-sec-card` (the shard/network breach splash) — which meant the admin card inherited `max-width:400px; width:90%; text-align:center; max-height; overflow-y:auto`. That produced "the card is squished to half width and everything is centered", was **misdiagnosed as a Foundry core 400px cap on content children (there is NO such rule — core's only `max-width:400px` is `.tour-center-step`)**, and got band-aided with `width:100% !important; max-width:none !important` while the centering/max-height stayed. Rules: (1) **before naming a new component, `grep -rn "\.ncm-<prefix>" styles/`** — if the prefix is taken, the NEWCOMER renames (shipped code keeps the name); (2) the admin-panel twin of a taken prefix takes an `-admin-` infix — `ncm-term-*` (terminal viewer) → `ncm-term-admin-*`, `ncm-sec-*` (breach overlays) → **`ncm-sec-admin-*`** (this tab); (3) don't scope generic names (`.tk`, `.tier`, `.state`) to the whole panel — nest them under the component.
- **Two Foundry core resets that bite any new admin surface** (both proven on the Security tab): core ships a bare **`table { margin: 1rem }`**, so any table without `margin: 0` sits shifted 16px right and its last column disappears under a card's `overflow:hidden` (the "↗ / Purge button is cut off" bug); and the module's own **`.ncm-app label { text-transform: uppercase }`** (base/typography.css, a display-font micro-caption) hits EVERY `<label>` — a checkbox label that's a sentence must opt out with `text-transform:none; font-family:var(--ncm-font-body); font-weight:400` or it renders "GM ALWAYS HAS ACCESS". Also: a `.ncm-x-frow > label` field-key rule will match a `<label class="checkbox">` sibling too — exclude it (`> label:not(.ncm-x-check)`).
- **A header row of flex children SHRINKS before it wraps.** Foundry/CPR give no min-widths, so an overcrowded toolbar squeezes its title/badge/chips into 2-3 line stacks rather than moving anything to a second row. Fix by making nothing shrink (`> * { flex: none }` + `white-space: nowrap` on the text bits) and letting the row `flex-wrap: wrap`. **Size it against the SCROLLBAR-PRESENT width**: the admin panel is 820×600 and a long tab always has a vertical scrollbar, so the real content box is ~15px narrower than a preview whose frame is tall enough to avoid one — a header that fits in the preview wraps in the app. `admin-security-preview.html` forces `overflow-y:scroll` for this reason.
- **Measure, don't grep — but make the measurement honest.** A faithful headless preview + `getComputedStyle` is the only reliable way to find these; `admin-security-preview.html?diag=1` reports overflow, wrap, computed `text-align`, and an inheritance chain. Three traps that made the diagnostic itself lie, all fixed there: a rule-matcher must **recurse into `@layer`/`@media` (`r.cssRules`) AND `@import` (`r.styleSheet.cssRules`)** — `night-city-messenger.css` is 57 imports, so a flat top-level scan finds *zero* module rules and every real culprit reads as "no matching rule"; `clientHeight` **includes padding**, so a padded cell reads as "wrapping" unless you subtract it; and flex children are center-aligned, so counting distinct `offsetTop`s reports one row per child — count row *bands* instead.
- **A preview that loads only the component's own CSS is worse than no preview** — it renders clean while the real app is broken, because every core/CPR reset above is absent. Always load the real stack: `foundry2.css` → CPR `main.css` → `night-city-messenger.css` → the component, and keep `.ncm-app` on the host. (`upload-console-preview.html` was component-only and was made faithful.)
- **A `:hover` transform that MOVES the element off the cursor causes hover flicker — and there are TWO tiers of fix (2026-08-14, took two rounds).** (1) An **isolated** mover (message/terminal rows' `translateX(2px)`, terminal `__btn`/`__chip` lift): keep the motion, add an invisible **`::after` hitbox guard** covering rest position + travel on the trailing edge — pseudo hits count as the element, so the hitbox never shrinks mid-move (rows verified stable under real CDP mouse events). (2) An element that **ANCHORS A DROPDOWN as a child across a gap** (the viewer's Dates/Sort/Filters buttons, `.ncm-sort-dropdown`/`.ncm-filter-dropdown` nested inside) must **NEVER translate on hover** — the transform moves the whole button+menu assembly, so crossing the 4px anchor gap creates an oscillating boundary NO guard can stabilize (CDP-measured 11 hover flips per traversal, with the message rows underneath hover-flashing through the gap slivers — the dev's "buttons glitch in and out / list flashes", reported twice because round 1 only applied fix 1). Dropdown anchors get color/border feedback only, **plus a `::before` gap bridge on the dropdown** (`top:-5px; height:5px` — the menus are static, so a bridge makes button→menu one continuous hover region; without it the gap still flashes the row beneath once per crossing). Same unguarded pattern remains on larger hover-movers (quickdial cards, workshop `pcard`, deck carts, terminal `__card`/`__rrow`/`__port`, claim/go buttons) — apply tier 1 if reported (check `::after` isn't already an effect). **Verify with real mouse events, not reasoning:** `tools/sims/cdp-hover.mjs` drives headless Chrome via CDP over `docs/mockups/refine-bar-flicker-harness.html` (full core+CPR+module cascade) and counts hover enter/leave storms — static-style assertions and `elementFromPoint` probes both missed the traversal storm.
- **A hard `min-height` on a flex child in a clipping column makes it overflow onto its sibling.** `.ncm-composer__editor` had `min-height: 120px` while being `flex: 1` inside the `flex:1` editor-wrap of an `overflow:hidden` composer column. When the composer is short (a reply whose quoted-message card eats vertical space), the wrap shrinks below 120px but the editor refuses to, so its box paints **down over the top of the footer's Send button** — and because the editor is `cursor: text !important`, that strip swallows clicks (only the button's bottom edge works; "top of the button won't click, cursor looks like text-select"). Fix: `min-height: 0` so the editor shrinks with its wrap; `flex:1` still fills when there's room. Do **not** "fix" it with `overflow:hidden` on the wrap — that clips the absolutely-positioned `.ncm-composer__overflow` "More tools" menu anchored inside it. (Diagnosed with `document.elementFromPoint` over the button in a headless-Chrome repro.)

## Conventions

- Console logging: use `log.info/warn/error/debug` from `scripts/utils/helpers.js`, not raw `console.*`. The `debug` channel respects the `debugMode` setting.
- BEM: `ncm-component__element--modifier`. Class prefix is `ncm-`.
- Fonts: Orbitron (UI chrome/titles), Rajdhani (body/display), Share Tech Mono (technical data values only).
- Cyberpunk RED lore: **Blackwall = red**, not purple.
- **NCM is NOT localized — copy lives inline in JS/HBS, and `languages/en.json` holds ONLY the 6 keys Foundry resolves for us** (5 `NCM.SceneControls.*` tooltips + `NCM.ContactManager.Title`). To change any string, grep the JS/templates, never the locale file. Cleaned up 2026-07-26: the file was 104 keys of which **84 were dead** and **9 were requested-but-absent** — and because Foundry auto-localizes a setting's `name`/`hint`, three `config: true` settings (`dateFormat`, `timeFormat`, `timeProvider`) were rendering the **raw key text** (`NCM.Settings.DateFormat.Name`) as their label in the GM's Module Settings menu. **Rule: register settings with LITERAL name/hint strings, never i18n keys** — all 54 now do (it was 38 literal / 16 keyed, and the mixed pairs, a keyed `name` beside a literal `hint`, are exactly how the three raw keys shipped unnoticed). Guard: **`node tools/audit-i18n.cjs`** cross-checks both directions (exit 1 on a requested-but-missing key, lists keys nothing asks for) and knows to check `settings.register` name/hint — run it after touching either side. `helpers.js` still exports an unused `localize()` as the entry point if real i18n is ever wanted.
- **Mock before visual code, and make the mock load the REAL cascade.** Standing dev preference. A mock file lives in `docs/mockups/`, is built from the real class vocabulary inside the real app root, and links Foundry core → CPR `main.css` → `night-city-messenger.css` (+ `admin-theme.css` for panel surfaces) — a component-only preview renders clean while the real app is broken. **A mockup that imports a real module CANNOT be opened off disk — serve it.** Chrome gives every `file://` URL its own opaque origin, so a `file://` page's ES-module `import` is refused as cross-origin, and the page renders with the imported thing simply missing: no error on screen, just a backdrop (the dev's "all I see is agent"). `--allow-file-access-from-files` fixes it for a headless render and does nothing for a double-click. **`node tools/serve-mockups.cjs`** (zero deps, localhost, read-only) serves the repo at `http://127.0.0.1:8123/` and fixes every preview at once, including the ones that `fetch` a real `.hbs`. Two rules for a preview that must work both ways: guard the import with a **dynamic** `await import()` in a try/catch that renders the fix on screen (a static import fails before any of your code runs, so there is nothing left to say it with), and pick the two out-of-repo stylesheets from `location.protocol` — `file:///…` off disk, the server's `/_vendor/…` mirror over http, because an http page may not load a `file://` stylesheet. Render it headless and *look at it* (`--headless --screenshot`, full `file:///` URL — a bare filename is treated as a hostname). Query params to switch states beat one static picture. **Four design lessons the malware-workshop rounds paid for, applicable to any new authoring surface:** (1) **card walls don't scale past ~6 options** in a 640px window — eleven 196px option cards ate the whole composer and pushed everything below the fold, the same mistake the 18-pill type wall made before it became a grouped dropdown; compact chips or a grouped `<select>` instead. (2) **A per-field hint belongs IN its field** (placeholder or a sub-line in the same cell), never stacked in a list under the grid where it orphans from what it describes. (3) **If every choice produces an identical screen, the surface reads as bland** — let a real property of the data drive the page (the workshop keys colour off the ability *family*, which also encodes what kind of harm it does). (4) **Don't default to dark-panel + one accent + a preview rail** — that's the shape of every other surface here and the generic AI-design default; find the structure in the subject (a malware program *lies*, so the page is the cover stacked on the payload).
- **In-world vocabulary belongs on PLAYER-FACING IMMERSIVE surfaces ONLY — GM/authoring UI uses plain language.** (Dev's rule, 2026-07-26.) A GM building content should never have to decode fiction: composer intros/labels/hints, config tabs, and admin panels stay in normal conventions ("a recovered chat transcript", not "a BabelChat scrape"). Setting-flavor words go where the *player* is in the fiction — a breach console, an action console, a rendered artifact. Exception worth knowing: a **placeholder or fallback that supplies in-world CONTENT** (the chatlog's "Chat app" field → `BabelChat`) is fine, because the authored value is what the player reads — but keep the placeholder and the enricher fallback identical or the field promises a name the render contradicts.
- **Canon vocabulary (setting flavor).** `docs/CANON-VOCAB-AND-IDEAS.md` is the adopt/consider/skip list, governed by [[ip-homebrew-constraint]] — **names and reworded concepts only, never verbatim descriptions/statblocks/tables**. Applied 2026-07-26 under the rule above: **Breacher** (the shard hack sequence's banner + status chip), **AltLink** (the exfil-to-Agent console intro), **BabelChat** (the chatlog `app` placeholder + enricher fallback). Available but **not** placed, for want of a player-facing home: **SAAI** (the Agent's assistant), ZPost/BangMe!/WatchBox. **Do NOT name the SAAI in the shared trace copy** — `NotificationService.showShardTrace` is the ONE toast for every trace source (shard/terminal/network/camera) and a SAAI belongs to an *Agent*, so it would misattribute 3 times in 4; doing it properly needs a `context` threaded through the trace payload the way `showShardBlackICE` already reads `data.context`. **Demon** is the right word for a *box's* automated defender if that ever gets built.
- All timestamps use UTC getters; stored ISO strings end in `Z`.
- **Simple Calendar's `currentDateTime()` returns `month` AND `day` as ZERO-BASED INDICES** (`getMonthAndDayIndex()` in its bundle). `Date.UTC`'s month is zero-based too but its day-of-month is 1-based, so `TimeService` passed the day index straight through and every NCM clock read **one day behind the calendar** in SC/Auto mode (tester 2026-10-20). The mapping is now the pure static `TimeService.scDateTimeToMs(dt)` (`day + 1`); sim `tools/sims/test-simple-calendar-date.mjs`. Mail stamped before the fix keeps its stored (one-day-early) timestamp — cosmetic, no migration. A non-Gregorian custom SC calendar can't map onto a JS Date at all; known limit.
- **All countdowns/expiries go through the time provider, not raw `Date.now()`.** Use `game.nightcity.timeService.getCurrentTime()` / `getCurrentTimeMs()` so timers respect the configured mode (real-time / world-time / manual / disguised). This covers self-destruct, network-access lockout (`MessageAccessService`), security/shard-hack lockouts (`SecurityService` — its auto-expiry is a poll-and-reschedule, not a single real-time `setTimeout`, so a paused game clock doesn't clear early), ICE breach lockout (`ContactBreachService`), the trace countdown, and the message "X ago" display. Only record timestamps and real-time animation legitimately use `Date.now()`.
- **Display dates via `formatCyberDate(iso, opts)` (helpers.js), never hand-format.** It honors three world settings: `dateFormat` (order — `YMD`/`DMY`/`MDY`), `dateMonthStyle` (month as `number` 03 / `short` Mar / `long` March, composes with the order), and `timeFormat` (24h/12h). Options: `{ dateOnly }`, `{ timeOnly }`, `{ seconds }`. The message-viewer HUD clock uses the default (date + time) at a reduced font so it fits the header. **CyberTimePicker follows all three too** (2026-07-13): its preview and the `formatted` string handed to `onSet` **delegate to `formatCyberDate` directly** (the old hand-built string ignored `dateMonthStyle` and could drift), and a written month style swaps the month digit-box for a named `<select>` (Jan/January, numeric values 1–12 so `_readInputs` parses either widget; the input listener is `'input change'` — selects need change). Presets: Game Time / Real Now / +1 Hour / +1 Day / +1 Week.
- CPR roles are actor items (`actor.items.filter(i => i.type === 'role')`), not `actor.system.role`.
- ICE actors detected by `type blackIce`/`black-ice`, flag `isBlackICE`, or name containing "black ice".
- Contact avatars resolve via `PortraitService.resolvePortrait`: `contact.portrait` → linked-actor `img` (when `contact.actorId` set) → `contact.customImg` → initials. When creating a contact programmatically (e.g. "Add" from a message), pass the sender's `actorId` and/or a `portrait` — name+email alone yields initials. `ContactRepository.addContact(actorId, contactData)` is `(ownerActorId, data)`; the contact's own linked actor goes in `data.actorId`.
- **"Burned" contacts are retired from scope but only CSS-hidden, not removed.** The burn/restore logic still lives in `MasterContactService` / `ContactRepository` / the tab + GM handlers. The UI is hidden via two reversible blocks marked `Burned UI hidden` in `styles/components/admin-panel/tab-contacts.css` (stat, filter pill, row badge, batch-burn, per-contact burn) and `styles/components/gm-contact-manager.css` (burn/restore buttons + `.ncm-chip--burned`). Delete those blocks to restore the UI. Burned references in the message viewer / email / toasts were intentionally left visible.

## Data layout

Inboxes are Foundry journals identified by **flags**, not name: `flags.night-city-messenger.{ type: 'inbox', actorId }` (actor inbox) or `{ type: 'contactInbox', contactId }` (contact inbox). Current/migrated worlds use **human-readable names** like `"V — Inbox"` / `"Fixer — Mail"`; the legacy `NCM-Inbox-<id>` / `NCM-Inbox-Contact-<id>` names are only a fallback.

**To scan `game.journal` for inboxes, ALWAYS use `MessageRepository.identifyInbox(journal)`** → `{ isContact, ownerId } | null` (flag-first, legacy-name fallback, skips archived inboxes in the Deleted folder). **Never key on the `NCM-Inbox-` name prefix** — a prefix scan silently matches nothing on current worlds, which is what made the admin Message Activity feed (and several other scans) come up empty. Callers: MessagesTab, MessageService, ContactsTab, CharacterSelectApp, MessageAccessService, DocumentSyncBridge.

Each message is a journal page with flags under the `night-city-messenger` namespace (`messageId`, `subject`, `body`, `status.read/deleted/spam/eddiesClaimed`, `timestamp`, `eddies`, `selfDestruct`, `references[]`, etc.). Sent copies have IDs ending in `-sent`. The four-subfolder hierarchy (Inboxes / NPC Mail / Data Shards / Deleted) under "Night City Messenger" is built and kept in sync by the migration tool in `MessageRepository.migrateJournalOrganization()`.

**Version-keyed migrations (2026-08-14): breaking flag-shape changes get an upgrade hook.** `scripts/core/MigrationRunner.js` + the hidden world setting `dataSchemaVersion` (default 0 = pre-versioning world) run at postReady/5, GM-only, no-op when current. To ship a breaking change: bump `DATA_SCHEMA_VERSION`, push `{to, label, run}` onto `MIGRATIONS` (idempotent — a crash mid-run reruns it; the version stamps after each successful step so later failures resume). Never renumber shipped entries. This exists because the alternative was permanent read-time back-compat or hand-run console snippets (the `uploadable:false` legacy-program episode). The two older shape-detecting migrations (`migrateJournalOrganization`, `MasterContactService._migrateContacts`) stay as they are — they key on detecting legacy data, not on a version.

## Compendium packs (macros)

The module ships two Macro compendiums declared in `module.json` `packs[]`:

- `packs/ncm-macros-player.db` — 6 player-safe macros, default ownership `OBSERVER`
- `packs/ncm-macros-gm.db` — 14 GM automation macros, default ownership `NONE`

Every macro body is a thin guarded wrapper over the existing `game.nightcity.messenger.*` API (`MacroAPI.js`) and carries `flags.night-city-messenger.{managed:true, sourceId, pack}`.

**Format is intentionally NeDB `.db`** (newline-delimited JSON, one Macro doc per line), not LevelDB. This keeps the "no build step / no `package.json`" rule literally true — Foundry v12 loads `.db` packs directly (with a *deprecation warning* in console; NeDB removal is slated for a future Foundry major). Migrating to LevelDB later is a one-time `fvtt package pack` run, deferred to a future-Foundry compat pass.

**Do not hand-edit the `.db` files.** They are generated. The source of truth is `tools/gen-macros.cjs` — a plain Node script Foundry never runs. After changing a macro, regenerate:

```bash
node tools/gen-macros.cjs
```

It re-emits both packs, validates every line round-trips as JSON, and parses every `command` the way Foundry runs it (an **async** function with `speaker, actor, token, character, scope` predefined — so don't declare locals named `actor`/`token`/`character`; use `_who` etc.). Macro bodies use only double-quoted strings + concatenation (no backticks/template literals) so they JSON-encode cleanly.

**Icons:** each macro's `img` is a fixed path `assets/icons/macros/<icon>.svg` (player icons cyan, GM icons neon-red; `assets/icons/macros/README.md` is a generated macro→file map). The generator seeds a neon placeholder SVG there **only when the file is missing** — it never overwrites existing art, so custom icons (same filename) survive every regen. Replacing a file in place takes effect immediately: no regen, no reinstall, Foundry just loads whatever is at the path. Keep the `.svg` extension (baked into the docs); to switch to PNG/WebP, change the extension/`ICON_WEB_BASE` in the generator, rerun, and reinstall via Admin → Tools.

**NeDB gotcha:** Foundry rewrites the module's `.db` pack files on disk when a world loads (NeDB compaction reorders lines and may add fields). This is harmless — the source of truth is `gen-macros.cjs`, and the Import/Reinstall flow copies into independent world Macro documents — but it means a post-load `.db` will not be line-for-line what the generator emitted. Diff packs **keyed by `_id`**, never by line order.

The packs never auto-write to a world. **Admin Panel → Tools → "Import / Reinstall Macros"** (`ToolsTab.onImportMacros`) is the explicit installer: it creates missing macros in world folders `NCM — Player Tools` / `NCM — GM Tools`, resets `managed:true` macros to the shipped version, and never touches macros the GM authored or detached (matched by `sourceId`).

Long-lived context about the developer, workflow, project state, and architectural decisions lives at `C:\Users\cphan\.claude\projects\C--Users-cphan-AppData-Local-FoundryVTT-Data-modules-night-city-messenger\memory\`. Read `MEMORY.md` for the index when something feels missing.
